, Remark 3.8]
.
Without the weak proregularity assumption, part (a) holds for
quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-modules.

Part (b) is essentially a result of Yekutieli;
see
[
60
, Theorem 1.6(1) or 6.9]
.
The validity of part (b) is equivalent to the weak proregularity of
the ideal
J
; see
[
42
, Theorem 7.2]
.

It is easy to deduce part (b) from the combination of part (a)
and Lemma
3.6
(c).
Indeed, let
P_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a projective resolution of a contraflat

J
-contramodule
F
in the abelian category
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
Then it is clear from part (a) that the complex
M\otimes_{S}P_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow M\otimes_{S}F\longrightarrow 0
is acyclic.
On the other hand, by Lemma
3.6
(c)
we have
\Tor^{S}_{n}(M,P)=0
for all projective objects

P\in S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
and all
n\geq 1
; so the complex

M\otimes_{S}P_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
computes the derived functor
\Tor^{S}_{*}(M,F)
.
∎

The following corollary is a further generalization of
Lemma
3.6
(c).

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
.
Let
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
and
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a bounded above complex of contraflat
J
-contramodule

S
-modules.
Then the complex of
S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
represents
the derived category object
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
In other words, the natural morphism

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muN^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}.

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Proof.

Let
P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a bounded above complex of projective objects in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
endowed with a quasi-isomorphism of complexes

P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
In view of Lemma
3.6
(c), we
only need to prove that the induced map of complexes

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
Indeed, denote by
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
the cone of the morphism of complexes

P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
So
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a bounded above acyclic complex of contraflat

J
-contramodule
S
-modules.
By Lemma
3.7
(a), the complex

M\otimes_{R}G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic for any
J
-torsion
S
-module
M
.
It follows that the complex
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic for any
finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
It remains to represent the given complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
as a direct limit of finite complexes of
J
-torsion

S
-modules, which can be done using the canonical truncations on
one side and the silly truncations on the other side, in order to
prove that the complex
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic.
∎

4.
Auslander and Bass classes

Let
S
be a commutative ring and
J\subset S
be a weakly proregular
finitely generated ideal.
Denote by
\mathfrak{S}=\varprojlim_{n\geq 0}S/J^{n}
the
J
-adic completion of
the ring
S
.

A
pseudo-dualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules satisfying
the following two conditions:

(ii)

for every finite complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with
J
-torsion cohomology modules, the complex
of
S
-modules
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded
above complex of finitely generated projective
S
-modules;

(iii)

the homothety map

\mathfrak{S}\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}[*])
is an isomorphism
of graded rings.

Assume that the finite complex
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-d_{1}\leq m\leq d_{2}
.
Choose an integer
l_{1}\geq d_{1}
, and consider the following full
subcategories in the abelian categories of
J
-torsion and

J
-contramodule
S
-modules:

•

\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}=\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is
the full subcategory consisting of all the
J
-torsion
S
-modules

E
such that
\Ext_{S}^{n}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)=0
for all
n>l_{1}
and the adjunction
morphism
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\longrightarrow E
is
an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}^{-}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
;

•

\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}=\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is
the full subcategory consisting of all the
J
-contramodule

S
-modules
F
such that
\Tor^{S}_{n}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},F)=0
for all
n>l_{1}
and
the adjunction morphism
F\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F)

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}^{+}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Clearly, for any
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}\geq l_{1}^{\prime}\geq d_{1}
, one has
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\subset\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\subset\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
The category
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
can be called the
Auslander class of

J
-contramodule
S
-modules
corresponding to a pseudo-dualizing
complex
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, while the category
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
is the
Bass class
of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
(cf.
[
39
, Section 3]

and
[
41
, Section 4]
).

Given an exact category
\mathsf{T}
, a full subcategory
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathsf{T}
is
said to be
coresolving
if
\mathsf{E}
is closed under extensions and
cokernels of admissible monomorphisms in
\mathsf{T}
, and for every object

T\in\mathsf{T}
there exists an admissible monomorphism
T\longrightarrow E
in
\mathsf{T}

with
E\in\mathsf{E}
.
Dually, a full subcategory
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{T}
is said to be

resolving
if
\mathsf{F}
is closed under extensions and kernels of
admissible epimorphisms in
\mathsf{T}
, and for every object
T\in\mathsf{T}

there exists an admissible epimorphism
F\longrightarrow T
in
\mathsf{T}
with

F\in\mathsf{F}
.
The following two lemmas imply that the full subcategory
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}

is coresolving in the abelian category
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, while
the full subcategory
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
is resolving in the abelian category

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

(a)
The full subcategory
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

is closed under the cokernels of injective morphisms, extensions, and
direct summands.

(b)
The full subcategory
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

is closed under the kernels of surjective morphisms, extensions, and
direct summands. ∎

The next lemma, which is our version of
[
39
, Lemma 3.2]

and
[
41
, Lemma 4.2]
, plays a key role.

Lemma 4.2
.

(a)
The full subcategory
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

contains all the injective objects of the abelian category

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

(b)
The full subcategory
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

contains all the contraflat
J
-contramodule
S
-modules.
In particular, all the projective objects of the abelian category

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
belong to
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
.

Proof.

Part (a): let
H
be an injective object of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Then, first of all, one has

\Ext^{n}_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)=H^{n}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)=0

for all
n\geq d_{1}
by
Lemma
3.6
(a).
It remains to check that the adjunction morphism

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)\longrightarrow H
is an isomorphism
in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Indeed, both
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)
and
H
are
complexes of
S
-modules with
J
-torsion cohomology modules
(see Lemma
3.3
(a)).
Let
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules with
J
-torsion cohomology modules.
By Lemma
3.2
(a),
it suffices to check that the morphism of complexes

(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muK^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}H

is a quasi-isomorphism.

By Lemma
3.1
and condition (ii),
there exists a bounded above complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
together with a quasi-isomorphism of complexes
of
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
For every complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, the complex of

S
-modules
\Hom_{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)
represents the derived category object

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)
by
Lemma
3.6
(a).
So we have a natural isomorphism

(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)=M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H)\\
\simeq\Hom_{S}(\Hom_{S}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),H)=\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),\>H)

in the derived category
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
Here we are using the fact that
\Hom_{S}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq\Hom_{S}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},S)\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
by Lemma
1.1
(a).

By condition (iii), the homothety map

\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathfrak{S})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
It remains to point out that the map

\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},S)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathfrak{S})

induced by the completion map
S\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}
is a quasi-isomorphism
of complexes of
S
-modules by
Lemma
3.1

and
[
36
, Lemma 5.4(b)]
.

Part (b): let
P
be a contraflat
J
-contramodule
S
-module.
Then, first of all, one has

\Tor_{n}^{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},P)=H^{-n}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}P)=0

for all
n\geq d_{1}
by
Corollary
3.8
.
It remains to check that the adjunction morphism

P\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}P)
is an isomorphism
in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Indeed, both
P
and
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}P)
are
complexes of
S
-modules with
J
-contramodule cohomology modules
(see Lemma
3.3
(b)).
Let
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules with
J
-torsion cohomology modules.
By Lemma
3.2
(b),
it suffices to check that the morphism of complexes

\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},P)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}P)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

As in part (a), we use Lemma
3.1

and condition (ii), and pick a bounded above complex of finitely
generated projective
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
together with
a quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
S
-modules

M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
For every complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, the complex of

S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}P
represents the derived category object

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}P
by
Corollary
3.8
.
So we have a natural isomorphism

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}P)=\Hom_{S}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}P)\\
\simeq\Hom_{S}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\otimes_{S}P=\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}P

in the derived category
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
Once again, we are using the fact that
\Hom_{S}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.
As in part (a), the argument finishes with the observations that
the maps

\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},S)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathfrak{S})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

are isomorphisms in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
by
[
36
, Lemma 5.4(b)]

and condition (iii).
∎

Remark 4.3
.

Similarly to
[
41
, Remark 4.3]
, we
do not know
whether
the analogue of
[
39
, Lemma 3.3]
holds in the context of
pseudo-dualizing complexes of
J
-torsion
S
-modules, i. e.,
whether the Bass class of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
is
always closed under infinite direct sums in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, and
whether the Auslander class of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules

\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
is always closed under infinite direct products
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
These questions are open even in the case of a Noetherian ring
S
,
when the class of injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules is closed under
infinite direct sums (since an object of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is injective
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
if and only if it is injective in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, as
one can see from the Artin–Rees lemma) and the class of contraflat

J
-contramodule
S
-modules is closed under infinite products (since
a
J
-contramodule
S
-module is contraflat if and only if it is flat
as an
S
-module; see
[
38
, Corollary 10.3(a)]
).

Lemma 4.4
.

(a)
Let
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
.
Then
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a complex of
J
-torsion

S
-modules concentrated in the cohomological degrees

-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
with the terms belonging to the full subcategory

\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
if and only if

\Ext_{S}^{n}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=0
for all
n>n_{2}+l_{1}
and the adjunction
morphism
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}^{-}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

(b)
Let
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
.
Then
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a complex of
J
-contramodule

S
-modules concentrated in the cohomological degrees

-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
with the terms belonging to the full subcategory

\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
if and only if

\Tor^{S}_{n}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=0
for
n>n_{1}+l_{1}
and the adjunction morphism

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}^{+}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Proof.

Part (a): The “only if” implication is obvious.
To prove the “if”, replace
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by a quasi-isomorphic complex

{}^{\prime}\!M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
concentrated in the same cohomological
degrees
-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
such that
{}^{\prime}!\!M^{m}
is an injective object
of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
for all
-n_{1}\leq m<n_{2}
.
Then use Lemma
4.2
(a)
in order to check that
{}^{\prime}\!M^{m}\in\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
for all

-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
.
Part (b): to prove the “if”, replace
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by a quasi-isomorphic
complex
{}^{\prime}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
concentrated in the same
cohomological degrees
-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
such that
{}^{\prime}Q^{m}
is
a projective object of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
for all
-n_{1}<m\leq n_{2}
.
Then use Lemma
4.2
(b)
in order to check that
{}^{\prime}Q^{m}\in\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
for all
-n_{1}\leq m\leq n_{2}
.
∎

It follows from
Lemma
4.4
(a) that
the full subcategory
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})\subset\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})

consists of all the complexes of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

with bounded cohomology such that the complex
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

also has bounded cohomology and the adjunction morphism

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is an isomorphism.
Similarly, by
Lemma
4.4
(b), the full
subcategory
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}})\subset\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

consists of all the complexes of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules

Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with bounded cohomology such that the complex

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
also has bounded cohomology and the adjunction
morphism
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
an isomorphism.

These two full subcategories can be called the
derived Bass
class of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
and the
derived Auslander
class of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
.
Any pair of adjoint functors between two categories restricts to
an equivalence between the full subcategories of objects whose
adjunction morphisms are isomorphisms
[
13
, Theorem 1.1]

(see also
[
14
, Proposition 2.1]
); so the functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
restrict to
a triangulated equivalence between the derived Bass and Auslander
classes

(10)

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}).

Lemma 4.5
.

(a)
For any
J
-torsion
S
-module
E\in\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
,
the object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
can be represented
by a complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-d_{2}\leq m\leq l_{1}
with the terms belonging
to
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
.

(b)
For any
J
-contramodule
S
-module
F\in\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
,
the object
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
can be represented by
a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-l_{1}\leq m\leq d_{2}
with the terms belonging
to
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
.

Proof.

Part (a) follows from
Lemma
4.4
(b), as
the derived category object
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\simeq E\in\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
has no cohomology in the cohomological degrees

-n<-d_{2}-l_{1}
(since
-d_{2}-l_{1}\leq-d_{2}-d_{1}\leq 0
).
Part (b) follows from
Lemma
4.4
(a), as
the derived category object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F)\simeq F\in\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
has no cohomology in the cohomological
degrees
n>d_{2}+l_{1}
(since
d_{2}+l_{1}\geq d_{2}+d_{1}\geq 0
).
∎

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a weakly idempotent-complete exact category (in
the sense of
[
6
, Section 7]
),
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathsf{T}
be a coresolving
subcategory, and
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{T}
be a resolving subcategory.
We refer to
[
57
, Section 2]
or
[
34
, Section A.5]
for
a discussion of the
\mathsf{E}
-coresolution dimensions
and
the
\mathsf{F}
-resolution dimensions
of the objects of
\mathsf{T}
.
The key point is that the (co)resolution dimension does not depend
on the choice of a (co)resolution
[
62
, Lemma 2.1]
,

[
57
, Proposition 2.3(1)]
,
[
34
, Corollary A.5.2]
.

Lemma 4.6
.

(a)
For any integers
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}\geq l_{1}^{\prime}\geq d_{1}
, the full
subcategory
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
consists precisely
of all the
J
-torsion
S
-modules whose
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime}}
-coresolution
dimension does not exceed
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}-l_{1}^{\prime}
.

(b)
For any integers
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}\geq l_{1}^{\prime}\geq d_{1}
, the full
subcategory
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
consists precisely of
all the
J
-contramodule
S
-modules whose
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime}}
-resolution
dimension does not exceed
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}-l_{1}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Part (a) follows from
Lemma
4.4
(a) applied
to a one-term complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=E
,
concentrated in the cohomological degree
0
, with the numerical
parameters
n_{1}=0
,
n_{2}=l_{1}^{\prime\prime}-l_{1}^{\prime}
, and
l_{1}=l_{1}^{\prime}
.
Part (b) similarly follows from
Lemma
4.4
(b) applied
to a one-term complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=F
,
concentrated in the cohomological degree
0
, with the numerical
parameters
n_{2}=0
,
n_{1}=l_{1}^{\prime\prime}-l_{1}^{\prime}
, and
l_{1}=l_{1}^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

It is clear from
Lemmas
4.2

and
4.6
that, for any integer

n\geq 0
, all the objects of injective dimension not exceeding
n
in
the abelian category
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
belong to
\mathsf{E}_{d_{1}+n}
and all
the
J
-contramodule
S
-modules of contraflat dimension not
exceeding
n
belong to
\mathsf{F}_{d_{1}+n}
.
Here the
contraflat dimension
of a
J
-contramodule

S
-module is simply defined as the resolution dimension with
respect to the resolving subcategory of contraflat
J
-contramodule

S
-modules in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
Clearly, the contraflat dimension of a
J
-contramodule
S
-module
never exceeds its projective dimension as a object of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

Proposition 4.8
.

(a)
For any integers
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}\geq l_{1}^{\prime}\geq d_{1}
and any
conventional or exotic derived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,

\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
,
\mathsf{bco}
, or
\mathsf{abs}
, the exact
inclusion functor
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}
induces
a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}).

(b)
For any integers
l_{1}^{\prime\prime}\geq l_{1}^{\prime}\geq d_{1}
and any
conventional or exotic derived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,

\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
,
\mathsf{bctr}
, or
\mathsf{abs}
, the exact
inclusion functor
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}
induces
a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}}).

Proof.

Part (b) follows from
Lemma
4.6
(b) in view
of
[
34
, Propositions A.5.8 and B.7.9]
.
Part (a) follows from
Lemma
4.6
(a) in view
the dual versions of
[
34
, Propositions A.5.8 and B.7.9]
.
∎

The cases
\star=\mathsf{bco}
and
\star=\mathsf{bctr}
in the context of
Proposition
4.8
are
actually trivial, and are only included in the formulation for the sake
of completeness and for comparison with
[
39
, Proposition 3.8]
.
Using the results of
[
48
, Corollary 9.5]
for

\mathsf{A}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
[
48
, Corollary 7.4]
for

\mathsf{B}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
, one can easily show that

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
and

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}^{\prime\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.

As a particular case of
Proposition
4.8
,
the conventional unbounded derived category of the Bass class of

J
-torsion
S
-modules
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})
is the same for all

l_{1}\geq d_{1}
, and the conventional unbounded derived category of
the Auslander class of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}})

is the same for all
l_{1}\geq d_{1}
.
Following the notation in
[
39
, Section 3]

and
[
41
, Section 4]
, we put

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}).

The next theorem, generalizing the triangulated
equivalence (
10
),
provides, in particular, a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}})=\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Theorem 4.9
.

For any conventional or absolute derived category symbol

\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,
\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
, or
\mathsf{abs}
,
there is a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.

Proof.

This is a particular case of
Theorem
5.2
below.
∎

Let us make some final comments before this section is finished.
According to
[
47
, Proposition 5.5]
, there is a natural
degenerate t-structure of the derived type on the triangulated
category
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})
with
the heart equivalent to
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Dual-analogously, by
[
47
, Proposition 5.7]
, there is a natural
degenerate t-structure of the derived type on the triangulated
category
\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}})
with
the heart equivalent to
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
See also the discussion in
[
41
, Section 1.2 and Remark 5.3]
.

Following the discussion in
[
41
, Section 2]
, the functor

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
induced
by the exact inclusion of exact/abelian categoires

\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is a triangulated Verdier quotient
functor having a (fully faithful) right adjoint.
Dual-analogously, the functor
\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}_{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
induced by the exact inclusion of exact/abelian
categories
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is a triangulated
Verdier quotient functor having a (fully faithful) left adjoint.
See also the discussion of diagram (
12
)
in the next Section
5
.

5.
Abstract Corresponding Classes

More generally, suppose that we are given two full subcategories

\mathsf{E}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{F}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
satisfying
the following conditions (for some fixed integers
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
):

(I)

the class of objects
\mathsf{E}
is closed under extensions and
cokernels of injective morphisms in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, and contains
all the injective objects of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
;

(II)

the class of objects
\mathsf{F}
is closed under extensions and
kernels of surjective morphisms in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
, and contains
all the projective objects of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
;

(III)

for any
J
-torsion
S
-module
E\in\mathsf{E}
, the derived category
object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\allowbreak\in\mathsf{D}^{+}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
can be
represented by a complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules concentrated
in the cohomological degrees
-l_{2}\leq m\leq l_{1}
with the terms belonging
to
\mathsf{F}
;

(IV)

for any
J
-contramodule
S
-module
F\in\mathsf{F}
, the derived
category object
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{-}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
can be represented
by a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-l_{1}\leq m\leq l_{2}
with the terms belonging
to
\mathsf{E}
.

Similarly to
[
39
, Section 4]
and
[
41
, Section 5]
,
one can see from conditions (I) and (III), or (II) and (IV), that

l_{1}\geq d_{1}
and
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
whenever

H^{-d_{1}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\neq 0\neq H^{d_{2}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
One also needs to use
Lemma
3.6
(a,c).

According to
Lemmas
4.1
,

4.2
,
and
4.5
,
the Bass and Auslander classes
\mathsf{E}=\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
and
\mathsf{F}=\mathsf{F}_{l_{2}}

satisfy conditions (I–IV) with
l_{2}=d_{2}
.
The following lemma can be viewed as providing a converse implication.

Lemma 5.1
.

(a)
For any
J
-torsion
S
-module
E\in\mathsf{E}
, the adjunction
morphism
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\longrightarrow E
is an isomorphism
in
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

(b)
For any
J
-contramodule
S
-module
F\in\mathsf{F}
,
the adjunction morphism
F\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F)

is an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Proof.

This is similar to
[
39
, Lemma 4.1]
and
[
41
, Lemma 5.1]
.
A direct argument along the lines of
[
39
, proof of Lemma 4.1]

is applicable, or alternatively, the assertions can be obtained from
(the proof of) Theorem
5.2
below.
In any case, the proof is based on
Lemmas
1.1
(a–b),

3.6
(a,c),
and
4.2
.
∎

Assuming that
l_{1}\geq d_{1}
and
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
, it is clear from
conditions (III–IV) and
Lemma
5.1

that the inclusions
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
and
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}

hold for any two classes of objects
\mathsf{E}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

and
\mathsf{F}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
satisfying (I–IV).
Furthermore, it follows from conditions (I–II) that the triangulated
functors
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
and

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{F})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
are fully faithful.
Hence the triangulated functors
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}})

and
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{F})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}})
are fully faithful, too.
Using again conditions (III–IV), we conclude that
the equivalence (
10
)
restricts to a triangulated equivalence

(11)

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{F}).

Let us introduce simplified notation

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}=(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})^{\mathsf{inj}}
and

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}}\allowbreak=(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})^{\mathsf{proj}}
for
the full subcategories of injective objects in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
projective objects in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

The following theorem is the first main result of this paper.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\mathsf{E}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{F}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

be a pair of full subcategories of
J
-torsion and
J
-contramodule

S
-modules satisfying conditions (I–IV) for a pseudo-dualizing
complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then, for any conventional or absolute derived category symbol

\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,
\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
, or
\mathsf{abs}
,
there is a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.

Proof.

The proof is completely similar to those of
[
39
, Theorem 4.2]

and
[
41
, Theorem 5.2]
.
The words “appropriately defined” here mean “produced by
the constructions of
[
39
, Appendix A]
”.
In the context of the latter, we set

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

\displaystyle\supset\mathsf{E}

\displaystyle\supset\mathsf{J}

\displaystyle=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}

\displaystyle=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

\displaystyle\supset\mathsf{F}

\displaystyle\supset\mathsf{P}

\displaystyle=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}}.

Consider the adjoint pair of DG-functors

\displaystyle\Psi=\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{C}^{+}(\mathsf{J})

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{C}^{+}(\mathsf{B}),

\displaystyle\Phi=L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}{-}\,

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{C}^{-}(\mathsf{P})

\displaystyle\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{C}^{-}(\mathsf{A})

(see Lemma
1.1
(a–b)).
Then the constructions of
[
39
, Sections A.2–A.3]
provide
the desired derived functors
\mathbb{R}\Psi\colon\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F})
and
\mathbb{L}\Phi\colon\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E})
.
According to
[
39
, Section A.4]
, the functor
\mathbb{L}\Phi
is
left adjoint to the functor
\mathbb{R}\Psi
.

Finally, the result of
[
39
, first assertion of Theorem A.5]

allows to deduce the claim that
\mathbb{R}\Psi
and
\mathbb{L}\Phi
are
mutually inverse equivalences from the particular case of
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
which is the triangulated
equivalence (
11
).
Alternatively, applying
[
39
, second assertion of Theorem A.5]

together with
Lemma
4.2
(and keeping
Lemma
3.6
(a,c) in mind) allows one
to reprove the triangulated
equivalence (
11
)
instead of using it, thus obtaining a proof
of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Let us make some comments generalizing the discussion at the end of
Section
4
.
According to
[
47
, Proposition 5.5]
, there is a natural
degenerate t-structure of the derived type on the triangulated
category
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})
with the heart equivalent to
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Dual-analogously, by
[
47
, Proposition 5.7]
, there is a natural
degenerate t-structure of the derived type on the triangulated
category
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})
with the heart equivalent to
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
See also the discussion in
[
41
, Section 1.2 and Remark 5.3]
.

The category of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is
a Grothendieck abelian categoyry.
Hence, by
[
54
, Theorem 3.13 and Lemma 3.7(ii)]
,

[
16
, Corollary 7.1]
, or
[
48
, Corollary 8.5]
, there are
enough homotopy injective complexes of injective objects in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
So the result of
[
41
, Theorem 2.1(a)]
is applicable, telling us
that the triangulated functor
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})

induced by the exact inclusion of exact/abelian categories

\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is a triangulated Verdier quotient
functor having a (fully faithful) right adjoint.

Dual-analogously, the category of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is a locally presentable (in fact, locally

\aleph_{1}
-presentable) abelian category with enough projective
objects.
Hence, by
[
48
, Lemma 6.1 and Corollary 6.7]
, there are enough
homotopy projective complexes of projective objects in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
So the result of
[
41
, Theorem 2.1(b)]
is applicable, telling us
that the triangulated functor
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

induced by the exact inclusion of exact/abelian categories

\mathsf{F}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is a triangulated Verdier quotient
functor having a (fully faithful) left adjoint.

In other words, we have a diagram of triangulated functors,
triangulated equivalences, commutativities, and adjunctions

(12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})}

with the notation and description very similar to the discussion
of the diagram (
7
) in the Introduction.
(Cf. the discussion in
[
41
, Section 9]
.)

The arrows that are present on both the diagrams (
7
)
and (
12
) denote the same functors.
The horizontal double line in (
12
) is
the triangulated equivalence from
Theorem
5.2
.
The downwards-directed straight arrows in the leftmost column
denote the triangulated functors between the homotopy/derived
categories induced by the exact inclusions of additive/exact/abelian
categories
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
The downwards-directed straight arrows in the rightmost column
denote the triangulated functors between the homotopy/derived
categories induced by the exact inclusions of additive/exact/abelian
categories
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{F}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

The upper levels of both the leftmost and the rightmost columns
in (
12
) are provided by
Theorem
2.2
.
The triangulated functors
\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
and
\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
are Verdier quotient functors in view of
Lemma
2.1

and Theorem
2.2
.

Now suppose that we have two pairs of full subcategories

\mathsf{E}_{\prime}\subset\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

\mathsf{F}_{\prime\prime}\subset\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
such that
both the pairs
(\mathsf{E}_{\prime},\mathsf{F}_{\prime\prime})
and

(\mathsf{E}^{\prime},\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime})
satisfy conditions (I–IV).
Then for every symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,
\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,

\mathsf{abs}-
, or
\mathsf{abs}
there is a commutative diagram of triangulated
functors and triangulated equivalences

(13)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.18753pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.18753pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 41.18753pt\raise-32.00891pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

The vertical functors are induced by the exact inclusions of exact
categories
\mathsf{E}_{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{F}_{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow\mathsf{F}^{\prime\prime}
, while the horizontal equivalences are provided by
Theorem
5.2
.

6.
Minimal Corresponding Classes

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a preudo-dualizing complex
of
J
-torsion
S
-modules concentrated in the cohomological
degrees
-d_{1}\leq m\leq d_{2}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Fix
l_{1}=d_{1}
and
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
.
Then there exists a unique minimal pair of full subcategories

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}=\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}=\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
satisfying
conditions (I–IV) from Section
5
.
For any pair of full subcategories
\mathsf{E}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

and
\mathsf{F}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
satisfying (I–IV), one has

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}\subset\mathsf{E}
and
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}\subset\mathsf{F}
.

Proof.

The full subcategories
\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
are constructed simultaneously
by a generation process similar to the ones in
[
39
, proof of
Proposition 5.1]
and
[
41
, proof of Proposition 6.1]
.
The difference is that,
like
in
[
41
]
and
unlike

in
[
39
]
, we do not require the classes
\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}
and
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}

to be closed under infinite direct sums and products.
Accordingly, no transfinite iterations of the generation process
are needed.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

Moreover, for any two integers
l_{1}\geq d_{1}
and
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
and
any two full subcategories
\mathsf{E}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

\mathsf{F}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
satisfying conditions (I–IV) with
the parameters
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
, one has
\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}\subset\mathsf{E}
and

\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}\subset\mathsf{F}
.
This can be easily seen from the construction of the classes

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}
and
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}
(cf.
[
39
, Remark 5.2]

and
[
41
, Remark 6.2]
).

One observes that the conditions (III–IV) become weaker as
the parameter
l_{2}
increases.
It follows that one has
\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}\supset\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}+1}
and

\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}\supset\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}+1}
for all
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
.
So the inclusions between our classes of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
and
J
-contramodule
S
-modules have the form

\displaystyle\dotsb\subset\mathsf{E}^{d_{2}+2}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{E}^{d_{2}+1}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{E}^{d_{2}}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{E}_{d_{1}}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{E}_{d_{1}+1}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{E}_{d_{1}+2}

\displaystyle\subset\dotsb\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}},

\displaystyle\dotsb\subset\mathsf{F}^{d_{2}+2}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{F}^{d_{2}+1}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{F}^{d_{2}}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{F}_{d_{1}}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{F}_{d_{1}+1}

\displaystyle\subset\mathsf{F}_{d_{1}+2}

\displaystyle\subset\dotsb\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
n\geq 0
,
l_{1}\geq d_{1}
, and
l_{2}\geq d_{2}+n
be some integers,
and let
\mathsf{E}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{F}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

be a pair of full subcategories satisfying conditions (I–IV) with
the parameters
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
.
Denote by
\mathsf{E}(n)\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
the full subcategory of
all
J
-torsion
S
-modules of
\mathsf{E}
-coresolution
dimension
\leq n
and by
\mathsf{F}(n)\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
the full
subcategory of all
J
-contramodule
S
-modules of
\mathsf{F}
-resolution
dimension
\leq n
.
Then the pair of classes of
J
-torsion and
J
-contramodule

S
-modules
\mathsf{E}(n)
and
\mathsf{F}(n)
satisfies conditions (I–IV)
with the parameters
l_{1}+n
and
l_{2}-n
.

Proof.

Similar to
[
39
, Lemma 5.3]
and
[
41
, Lemma 6.3]
.
∎

Proposition 6.4
.

(a)
For any integers
l_{2}^{\prime\prime}\geq l_{2}^{\prime}\geq d_{2}
and any
conventional or exotic derived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,

\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
,
\mathsf{bco}
, or
\mathsf{abs}
, the exact
inclusion functor
\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime}}
induces
a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}}).

(b)
For any integers
l_{2}^{\prime\prime}\geq l_{2}^{\prime}\geq d_{2}
and any
conventional or exotic derived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,

\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
,
\mathsf{bctr}
, or
\mathsf{abs}
, the exact
inclusion functor
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime}}
induces
a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime}}).

Proof.

Similar to
[
39
, Proposition 5.4]
and
[
41
, Proposition 6.4]
.
In part (b), one uses
Lemma
6.3
in order to check
that the
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}}
-resolution dimension of any object from

\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime}}
does not exceed
l_{2}^{\prime\prime}-l_{2}^{\prime}
.
Then one applies
[
34
, Propositions A.5.8 and B.7.9]
,
as in the proof of
Proposition
4.8
.
In part (a), one similarly uses
Lemma
6.3
in order to check
that the
\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}}
-coresolution dimension of any object from

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime}}
does not exceed
l_{2}^{\prime\prime}-l_{2}^{\prime}
.
Then one applies the dual versions
of
[
34
, Propositions A.5.8 and B.7.9]
.
∎

As in Proposition
4.8
,
the cases
\star=\mathsf{bco}
and
\star=\mathsf{bctr}
in the context of
Proposition
6.4
are
actually trivial, and are only included in the formulation for the sake
of completeness and for comparison with
[
39
, Proposition 5.4]
.
Using the results of
[
48
, Corollary 9.5]
for

\mathsf{A}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
[
48
, Corollary 7.4]
for

\mathsf{B}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
, one can easily show that

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}^{\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
and

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}^{\prime}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.

As a particular case of
Proposition
6.4
,
the conventional unbounded derived category
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})
of
the minimal corresponding class of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}
is the same for all
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
, and the conventional
unbounded derived category
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}})
of the minimal
corresponding class of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}

is the same for all
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
.
We put

\mathsf{D}_{\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{D}_{\prime\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}).

The next theorem provides, in particular, a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}_{\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}})=\mathsf{D}_{\prime\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Theorem 6.5
.

For any conventional or absolute derived category symbol

\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,
\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
, or
\mathsf{abs}
,
there is a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.

Proof.

This is another particular case of
Theorem
5.2
.
∎

Similarly to the discussion at the end of
Section
4
, and as a particular case
of the discussion in Section
5
, we mention
the following observations.
According to
[
47
, Proposition 5.5]
, there is a natural
degenerate t-structure of the derived type on the triangulated
category
\mathsf{D}_{\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})
with
the heart equivalent to
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Dual-analogously, by
[
47
, Proposition 5.7]
, there is a natural
degenerate t-structure of the derived type on the triangulated
category
\mathsf{D}_{\prime\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}})

with the heart equivalent to
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
See also the discussion in
[
41
, Section 1.2 and Remark 5.3]
.

Following the discussion in
[
41
, Section 2]
, the functor

\mathsf{D}_{\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})

induced by the exact inclusion of exact/abelian categoires

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is a triangulated Verdier quotient
functor having a (fully faithful) right adjoint.
Dual-analogously, the functor

\mathsf{D}_{\prime\prime}^{L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

induced by the exact inclusion of exact/abelian categories

\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is a triangulated Verdier quotient
functor having a (fully faithful) left adjoint.
See also the diagrams (
7
) in the Introduction
and (
12
) in Section
5
.

7.
Finiteness Conditions for an Ideal with Artinian Quotient Ring

Let
S
be a Noetherian commutative ring and
J\subset S
be an ideal
such that the quotient ring
S/J
is Artinian.
The aim of this section is to compare two finiteness conditions on
a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules: viz., condition (ii)
from the definition of a pseudo-dualizing complex in
Section
4
above and condition (iii)
from the definition of a dedualizing complex in
[
36
, Section 4]
.

Let
\sqrt{J}\subset S
denote the radical of the ideal
J
.
Notice that the quotient ring
S/\sqrt{J}
is a semisimple Artinian
commutative ring, i. e., a finite direct sum of fields.

Given a
J
-torsion
S
-module
M
, denote by
\soc(M)\subset M

the socle of
M
, i. e., the maximal semisimple submodule of
M
.
Equivalently,
\soc(M)
is the maximal
S
-submodule of
M
whose

S
-module structure comes from an
S/\sqrt{J}
-module structure.
It follows that one has
\soc(M)\neq 0
whenever
M\neq 0
.

So
M\longmapsto\soc(M)
is a functor
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow(S/\sqrt{J}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
The functor
\soc\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow(S/\sqrt{J}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is right
adjoint to the identity inclusion functor
(S/\sqrt{J}){\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
f\colon M\longrightarrow N
be a morphism of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.
Then the morphism
f
is injective if and only if the morphism

\soc(f)\colon\soc(M)\longrightarrow\soc(N)
is injective.

Proof.

The functor
\soc
is a right adjoint, hence it is left exact, i. e.,
preserves kernels.
Thus we have
\ker(\soc(f))=\soc(\ker(f))
.
As
\ker(f)
is a
J
-torsion
S
-module, we have
\ker(f)\neq 0

if and only if
\soc(\ker(f))\neq 0
.
∎

Recall that a
J
-torsion
S
-module is injective in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
if and only if it is injective in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
(since
the ring
S
is Noetherian).
A complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to be

minimal
if the differential of the complex
\soc(J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

vanishes.

Lemma 7.2
.

Any complex of injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules decomposes as
a direct sum of a minimal complex of injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules
and a contractible complex of injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules.

Proof.

Let
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a bounded below complex of injective
J
-torsion

S
-modules.
For every integer
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
T_{n}\subset\soc(H^{n})
be
a complementary submodule to the kernel of the map
\soc(H^{n})\longrightarrow\soc(H^{n+1})
; so
T_{n}
is a maximal submodule among all submodules

T\subset\soc(H^{n})
such that the composition
T\longrightarrow\soc(H^{n})\longrightarrow\soc(H^{n+1})
is injective.
In other words, the map from
T_{n}
to the image of the morphism

\soc(H^{n})\longrightarrow\soc(H^{n+1})
is an isomorphism.
Denote by
K_{n}
the injective envelope of
T_{n}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
,
or equivalently, in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Then the inclusion
T_{n}\longrightarrow\soc(H^{n})\longrightarrow H^{n}
can be extended to
an injective map of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
K_{n}\longrightarrow H^{n}
.
We have
\soc(K_{n})=T_{n}
, so it follows from
Lemma
7.1
that the composition

K_{n}\longrightarrow H^{n}\longrightarrow H^{n+1}
is an injective map.

We have constructed an injective morphism into the complex of

S
-modules
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
from a contractible two-term complex of injective

J
-torsion
S
-modules
\dotsb\longrightarrow 0\longrightarrow K_{n}\overset{\mathrm{id}}{\longrightarrow}K_{n}\longrightarrow 0\longrightarrow\dotsb
situated in the cohomological degrees
n

and
n+1
.
Now the composition
K_{n}\longrightarrow H^{n+1}\longrightarrow H^{n+2}
vanishes,
while the composition
K_{n+1}\longrightarrow H^{n+1}\longrightarrow H^{n+2}
is
injective, too.
It follows that the images of
K_{n}
and
K_{n+1}
do not intersect in

H^{n+1}
, so the map
K_{n}\oplus K_{n+1}\longrightarrow H^{n+1}
is injective.
We have arrived to an injective morphism of complexes

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(K_{n}\overset{\mathrm{id}}{\to}K_{n})\longrightarrow H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Contractible complexes of injective objects are injective objects of
the abelian category of complexes
\mathsf{C}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
;
hence the complex
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
decomposes into a direct sum of the complex

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and some complex of injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
One can easily see that the morphism of complexes
\soc(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow\soc(H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
induces an isomorphism on the images of the differentials;
so the complex
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is minimal.
∎

The following theorem is the main result of this section.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
S
be a Noetherian commutative ring and
J\subset S
be
an ideal such that the quotient ring
S/J
is Artinian.
Let
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.
Then the following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

the cohomology
S
-modules of the complex
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are Artinian;

(2)

for every finite complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with
J
-torsion cohomology modules,
the complex of
S
-modules
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic
to a bounded above complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules.

Proof.

(1)
\Longrightarrow
(2)
It follows from
[
36
, Lemma 4.3]
that
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic
to a finite complex of Artinian
J
-torsion
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then the complex of
S
-modules
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
quasi-isomorphic to the complex
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},S)\otimes_{S}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, which is also a finite complex of
Artinian
J
-torsion
S
-modules.

Clearly, there exists an integer
n\geq 1
such that all the elements
of
J^{n}\subset S
act on the complex of
S
-modules
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by
endomorphisms homotopic to zero.
Then the cohomology modules of the complex
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are
annihilated by
J^{n}
.

Any Artinian
S
-module
H
annihilated by
J^{n}
is an Artinian module
over the Artinian ring
S/J^{n}
, and it follows that the
S
-module
H

is finitely generated.
Any finite complex of modules
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
over a Noetherian ring
S
with
finitely generated cohomology modules
H^{i}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded above complex of finitely generated
projective
S
-modules.

(2)
\Longrightarrow
(1)
As any bounded below complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules, the complex

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to some bounded below complex of injective

J
-torsion
S
-modules
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
By Lemma
7.2
, we can assume
without loss of generality that the complex
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is minimal.
Using the canonical truncation, we construct from
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
a finite
minimal complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
quasi-isomorphic
to
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Let
\mathbf{s}=(s_{1},\dotsc,s_{m})
be a finite sequence of generators of
the ideal
\sqrt{J}\subset S
.
Then the dual Koszul complex
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})
is a finite complex
of finitely generated free
S
-modules with
J
-torsion cohomology
modules.
In fact, every element of
\sqrt{J}\subset S
acts on
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by
an endomorphism homotopic to zero.

The finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is minimal,
since
\soc(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}\soc(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a complex
with zero differential.
Every element of
\sqrt{J}
acts on
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by an endomorphism
homotopic to zero, so the cohomology modules of
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are

S/\sqrt{J}
-modules (i. e., semisimple
J
-torsion
S
-modules).
Furthermore, the complex of
S
-modules
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic
to
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
By (2), the complex
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic to
a bounded above complex of finitely generated projective
S
-modules.
Thus the cohomology modules of
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are finitely generated
semisimple
J
-torsion
S
-modules.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
be the minimal integer such that the term
C^{n}
of
the complex
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
not
an Artinian
S
-module.
Then, by
[
36
, Lemma 4.1]
, the
S
-module
\soc(C^{n})
is

not
finitely generated.
The complex
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is minimal, so the composition
\soc(C^{n})\longrightarrow C^{n}\longrightarrow C^{n+1}
vanishes.
Hence
\soc(C^{n})
is an infinitely generated semisimple submodule
of the kernel
Z^{n}
of the differential
C^{n}\longrightarrow C^{n+1}
.
Thus the
S
-module
Z^{n}
is
not
Artinian.
On the other hand, by the choice of
n
, the
S
-module
C^{n-1}

is Artinian.
It follows that the cokernel of the differential
C^{n-1}\longrightarrow Z^{n}

is not Artinian.
This cokernel is the degree
n
cohomology module
H^{n}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
of
the complex
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, and we have seen in the previous paragraph
that the
S
-module
H^{n}(C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is finitely generated and semisimple.
The contradiction proves that an integer
n
does not exist, i. e.,
all the terms of the complex
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are Artinian
S
-modules.

As
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\simeq K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a nonzero
finite complex of finitely generated free
S
-modules, we arrive
to the conclusion that all the terms of the complex
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are
Artinian
J
-torsion
S
-modules, implying (1).
∎

8.
Dedualizing Complexes

In this section we establish a comparsion of the definitions of
dedualizing complexes from
[
36
, Section 4]

and
[
36
, Section 5]
, thus answering a question that was left
open in the paper
[
36
]
.
We also deduce the triangulated equivalences of
[
36
, Theorems 4.9
and 5.10]
as particular cases of
Theorem
4.9
above.

Let
\mathsf{E}
be an exact category.
A finite complex
E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
\mathsf{E}
is said to have
projective
dimension
\leq\penalty d
if
\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E})}(E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M[n])=0
for
all objects
M\in\mathsf{E}
and all integers
n>d
.
Dually, the complex
E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to have
injective
dimension
\leq\penalty d
if
\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(\mathsf{E})}(M,E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}[n])=0
for
all objects
M\in\mathsf{E}
and all integers
n>d
.
Let us denote the projective dimension of
E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
as a complex
in
\mathsf{E}
by
\ppd_{\mathsf{E}}(E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
and the injective dimension of
E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

as a complex in
\mathsf{E}
by
\iid_{\mathsf{E}}(E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
.
A finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to have

projective dimension
\leq\penalty d
if
\Ext^{n}_{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M)=0

for all
J
-torsion
S
-modules
M
and all integers
n>d
.
Following
[
36
, Section 4]
, we denote the projective dimension of

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
as a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules by

\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
In view of
Theorem
3.4
or
Lemma
3.6
(a), the projective
dimension of
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
as per the definition above is equal to its
projective dimension as a complex in the abelian category

\mathsf{E}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
(which was the definition of the projective
dimension of a finite complex of torsion modules
in
[
36
, Section 5]
).
So we have
\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\ppd_{S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

We will say that a finite complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules

Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has
injective dimension
\leq\penalty d
if

\Ext^{n}_{S}(P,Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=0
for all
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
P
and
all integers
n>d
.
We denote the injective dimension of
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
as a complex of

J
-contramodule
S
-modules by
\iid_{(S,J)}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
In view of
Theorem
3.5
or
Lemma
3.6
(b), the injective
dimension of
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
as per the definition above is equal to its
injective dimension as a complex in the abelian category

\mathsf{E}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
So we have
\iid_{(S,J)}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\iid_{S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

A finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to have

contraflat dimension
\leq\penalty d
if
\Tor_{n}^{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},P)=0

for all
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
P
and all integers
n>d
.
In view of Lemma
3.6
(c) (see also
Corollary
3.8
),
this definition of the contraflat dimension of a finite complex of
torsion modules agrees with the one in
[
36
, Section 5]
.
Following
[
36
, Section 4]
, we denote the contraflat dimension of

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by
\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
It is clear from the formula
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(\Tor_{n}^{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},P),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\simeq\Ext^{n}_{S}(P,\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))
that the contraflat
dimension of
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is equal to the injective dimension of the finite
complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
.
So we have
\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\iid_{(S,J)}\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
.

A finite complex of
S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to have

flat dimension
\leq\penalty d
if
\Tor_{n}^{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M)=0

for all
S
-modules
M
and all integers
n>d
.
We denote the flat dimension of
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by
\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Using the formula
\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(\Tor_{n}^{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\simeq\Ext^{n}_{S}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))
, one can easily show
that the flat dimension of
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
does not exceed its projective
dimension as a complex in the abelian category
\mathsf{E}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
We also put
\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\ppd_{S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
\iid_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\iid_{S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
So we have
\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
One can also easily see that
\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\iid_{S}\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
.

Let
\mathfrak{S}=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}S/J^{n}
be the
J
-adic completion of
the ring
S
.
In the case of a Noetherian ring
S
, the arguments
from
[
36
, Proposition 4.7 and its proof]
are applicable,
and one obtains the equations and inequalities

\fd_{\mathfrak{S}}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

for any finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

(use an injective cogenerator of the category
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

in place of the module
C
in the context of
[
36
, proof of
Proposition 4.7]
).
The following lemma provides somewhat weaker inequalities for
a non-Noetherian ring
S
.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
s_{1}
, …,
s_{m}
be a finite set
of generators of the ideal
J\subset S
.
Let
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules and

Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules.
Then one has

(a)

\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
;

(b)
