\iid_{(S,J)}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\iid_{S}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\iid_{(S,J)}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
;

(c)

\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
;

(d)

\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
.

Proof.

Part (a): the inequality
\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
follows
immediately from the definitions.
To prove the inequality
\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
, put

\mathbf{s}=(s_{1},\dotsc,s_{m})
, and let
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})
be the infinite
dual Koszul complex from Section
1
.
Let
\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\subset\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
be the full subcategory of
all complexes with
J
-torsion cohomology modules in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
Following, e. g., the discussion in
[
42
, Section 2]
,
the functor
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}{-}\,\colon\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
is right adjoint to the inclusion functor

\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
So for any
S
-module
M
we have
\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},M)\simeq\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}M)
.
It remains to point out that
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}M
is
a finite complex of
S
-modules with
J
-torsion cohomology modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
\leq m
.

Part (b): the inequality
\iid_{(S,J)}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\iid_{S}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
follows
immediately from the definitions.
To prove the inequality
\iid_{S}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\iid_{(S,J)}Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
, keep
the notation
\mathbf{s}
from the proof of part (a), and let
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})

be the finite complex of countably generated projective
S
-modules
from Section
1
.
Let
\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\subset\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
be the full subcategory
of all complexes with
J
-contramodule cohomology modules in

\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
Following the discussion in
[
42
, Section 2]
, the functor

\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),{-})\colon\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})

is left adjoint to the inclusion functor
\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
So for any
S
-module
M
we have
\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})}(M,Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq\Hom_{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})}(\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),M),Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
It remains to point out that
\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),M)
is
a finite complex of
S
-modules with
J
-contramodule cohomology
modules concentrated in the cohomological degrees
\geq-m
.

Part (c): the inequality
\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
follows
immediately from the definitions.
To prove the inequality
\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
, we use
the equalities
\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\iid_{S}\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
and

\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\iid_{(S,J)}\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
.
Then it remains to apply part (b) to the complex of
J
-contramodule

S
-modules
Q^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{Z}}(N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
.

Part (d) is provable by comparing parts (a) and (c).
One has
\cfd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\fd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}+m
.
∎

A
dedualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a pseudo-dualizing complex (according to
the definition in Section
4
) satisfying
the following additional condition:

(i)

the complex
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has finite projective dimension as
a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules, that is,

\ppd_{(S,J)}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}<\infty
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of

J
-torsion
S
-modules.
Assume that
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has a finite projective dimension as a complex
of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.
Let
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules.
Assume that the complex of
S
-modules
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded above complex of finitely generated
projective
S
-modules.
Then the complex
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is actually quasi-isomorphic
to a finite complex of finitely generated projective
S
-modules.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.1
(a), it
follows from the first assumption of the present lemma that the complex

N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has finite projective dimension as a complex in the abelian
category
\mathsf{E}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, that is
\ppd_{S}N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}<\infty
.
This means that
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of
(infinitely generated) projective
S
-modules.
Therefore, the complex
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is also quasi-isomorphic
to a finite complex of projective
S
-modules.
As
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is also quasi-isomorphic to a bounded above
complex of finitely generated projective
S
-modules by assumption,
it follows that
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite
complex of finitely generated projective
S
-modules.
∎

Now we can establish the comparisons of our definition of a dedualizing
complex of torsion modules with the definitions in
[
36
, Sections 4
and 5]
.

Corollary 8.3
.

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
and
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
J
-torsion

S
-modules.
Then
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a dedualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
in the sense of the definition above if and only if
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
a dedualizing complex for the ideal
J\subset S
in the sense of
the definition in
[
36
, Section 5]
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.1
(d),
finiteness of the projective dimension
\ppd_{(S,J)}B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
implies
finiteness of the contraflat dimension
\cfd_{(S,J)}B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
So the homological dimension condition (i) above is equivalent to
the homological dimension condition (i) from
[
36
, Section 5]
.
The homothety isomorphism condition (iii) from
Section
4
above is equivalent to
the homothety isomorphism condition (ii) from
[
36
, Section 5]
in
view of Theorem
3.4
.

Finally, by
[
36
, Proposition 5.1]
, a complex of
S
-modules

C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with
J
-torsion cohomology modules is a compact object of

\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
if and only if
C^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to
a finite complex of finitely generated projective
S
-modules.
In view of
Lemma
8.2
,
it follows that the finiteness condition (ii) from
Section
4
above is equivalent to
the finiteness condition (iii) from
[
36
, Section 5]
under
the assumption of the homological dimension condition (i).
∎

Corollary 8.4
.

Let
S
be a Noetherian commutative ring and
J\subset S
be an ideal
such that the quotient ring
S/J
is Artinian.
Let
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.
Then
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a dedualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
in the sense of the definition above if and only if
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
a dedualizing complex for the ideal
J\subset S
in the sense of
the definition in
[
36
, Section 4]
.

Proof.

The homological dimension condition (i) above coincides with
the homological dimension condition (i) from
[
36
, Section 4]
.
The homothety isomorphism condition (iii) from
Section
4
above is equivalent to
the homothety isomorphism condition (ii) from
[
36
, Section 4]
in
view of Theorem
3.4

(we recall that all ideals in a Noetherian commutative ring are
weakly proregular; see Section
1
).

Finally, the finiteness condition (ii) from
Section
4
above is equivalent to
the finiteness condition (iii) from
[
36
, Section 4]
by
Theorem
7.3
.
∎

Now we can conclude that the definition of a dedualizing complex
from
[
36
, Section 4]
agrees with the one
from
[
36
, Section 5]
.
This question was left open in the paper
[
36
]
;
see
[
36
, Remark 5.9]
.

Corollary 8.5
.

Let
S
be a Noetherian commutative ring and
J\subset S
be an ideal
such that the quotient ring
S/J
is Artinian.
Let
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.
Then
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a dedualizing complex for the ideal
J\subset S
in
the sense of the definition in
[
36
, Section 4]
if and only if

B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a dedualizing complex for the ideal
J\subset S
in
the sense of the definition in
[
36
, Section 5]
.

Proof.

Compare Corollary
8.3

with Corollary
8.4
.
∎

Finally, we proceed to obtain the triangulated equivalences
of
[
36
, Theorems 4.9 and 5.10]
as particular cases of
Theorem
4.9
above.

Let
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dedualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-d_{1}\leq m\leq d_{2}
.
Let us choose the parameter
l_{1}
in such a way that both
the projective and the contraflat dimensions of
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
do not
exceed
l_{1}
, that is
\ppd_{(S,J)}B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq l_{1}
and

\cfd_{(S,J)}B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\leq l_{1}
(this is possible by condition (i)
and Lemma
8.1
(d)).
One can see that any one of these two conditions implies
l_{1}\geq d_{1}

if
H^{-d_{1}}(B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\neq 0
(take
M
to be an injective cogenerator of

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
or
P=\mathfrak{S}
in the definitions of the projective and
contraflat dimensions).

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dedualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules,
and let the parameter
l_{1}
be chosen as stated above.
Then the related Bass and Auslander classes

\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}=\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}(B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
and
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}=\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}(B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

coincide with the whole categories of
J
-torsion
S
-modules and

J
-contramodule
S
-modules,
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
5.1

and the subsequent discussion, it suffices ot check that conditions
(I–IV) of Section
5
hold for the classes

\mathsf{E}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{F}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
with the given
parameter
l_{1}
and some integer
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
.
Indeed, let us take
l_{2}=d_{2}
.
Then conditions (I–II) are obvious, and conditions (III–IV) follow
from (i) and Lemma
8.1
(d)
(or from the choice of
l_{1}
).
∎

It is clear from Lemma
8.6

that for a dedualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

one has

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}_{B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Corollary 8.7
.

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
B^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dedualizing complex of

J
-torsion
S
-modules.
Then, for any conventional or absolute derived category symbol

\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,
\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
, or
\mathsf{abs}
,
there is a triangulated equivalence
\mathsf{D}^{\star}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
provided by (appropriately defined)
mutually inverse derived functors
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\penalty{-}
.

Proof.

This is a restatement of
[
36
, Theorem 5.10]
(in view of
Corollary
8.3
),
a generalization of
[
36
, Theorem 4.9]
(in view of
Corollary
8.4
), and
a particular case of Theorem
4.9

above (in view of Lemma
8.6
).
∎

9.
Adically Coherent Rings and Coherent Torsion Modules

We start with a general ring-theoretic lemma
[
19
, Lemma 1
and Theorem 2]
.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
A
be an associative ring and
I\subset A
be a two-sided ideal.
Assume that
I
is finitely generated as a left
A
-module.
In this context:

(a)
a left
A/I
-module is finitely presented if and only if
it is finitely presented as a left
A
-module;

(b)
if the ring
A
is left coherent, then so is
the ring
A/I
.

Proof.

Part (a): let
M
be a finitely presented left
A/I
-module;
so
M
is the cokernel of a morphism of finitely generated projective
left
A/I
-modules
Q_{1}\longrightarrow Q_{0}
.
Then both
Q_{1}
and
Q_{0}
are finitely presented left
A
-modules
(since
A/I
is a finitely presented left
A
-module); so
M
is
the cokernel of a morphism of finitely presented left
A
-modules.
Thus
M
is finitely presented as a left
A
-module.

The converse implication does not depend on the assumtion that
I

is finitely generated.
Let
M
be a left
A/I
-module that is finitely presented as
a left
A
-module.
So
M
is the cokernel of a morphism of finitely generated projective
left
A
-modules
f\colon P_{1}\longrightarrow P_{0}
.
Then
M
is also the cokernel of the morphism of finitely generated
projective left
A/I
-modules
A/I\otimes_{A}f\colon A/I\otimes_{A}P_{1}\longrightarrow A/I\otimes_{A}P_{0}
.

Part (b): let
J\subset A/I
be a finitely generated left ideal.
Then there is a finitely generated left ideal
K\subset A
such that

J=(K+I)/I
(lift any finite set of generators of
J
to some elements
of
A
and generate the ideal
K
by the resulting elements).
Since
I
is a finitely generated left
A
-module by assumption,
the ideal
K+I\subset A
is also finitely generated.
By assumption, it follows that
K+I
is a finitely presented
left
A
-module.
As
I
is a finitely generated left
A
-module, it follows that

J=(K+I)/I
is a finitely presented left
A
-module.
Using part (a), we can conclude that
J
is a finitely presented
left
A/I
-module, as desired.
∎

Remark 9.2
.

Let
I\subset A
be a nilpotent ideal; so
I^{n}=0
for some
n\geq 1
.
Assume that
I
is finitely generated as a left
A
-module.
Then one can easily see that the ring
A
is left Noetherian
whenever the ring
A/I
is left Noetherian.
The analogous assertion for coherent rings is
not
true.
For example, let
k
be a field and
k[s;x_{1},x_{2},\dotsc]
be
the commutative ring of polynomials in a countable family of variables

x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, … and an additional variable
s
over
k
.
Let
A
be the quotient ring of
k[s;x_{1},x_{2},\dotsc,]
by
the ideal spanned by the elements
s^{2}
and
sx_{n}
,
n\geq 1
.
Let
I=(s)\subset A
be the principal ideal spanned by
the element
s
.
Then
I^{2}=0
and
A/I=k[x_{1},x_{2},\dotsc]
is the ring of polynomials
in the countable family of variables
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, … over
k
;
so the ring
A
is coherent.
But the ring
A
is
not
coherent, since the ideal
I=(s)
is
not finitely presented as an
A
-module.

Let
\mathsf{T}
be a category with direct limits.
We recall that an object
M\in\mathsf{T}
is said to be
finitely
presented
if the functor
\Hom_{\mathsf{T}}(M,{-})\colon\mathsf{T}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sets}

preserves direct limits
[
1
, Definition 1.1]
.
The category
\mathsf{T}
is called
locally finitely presentable
if
all colimits exist in
\mathsf{T}
and there is a set of finitely presented
objects
\mathsf{S}\subset\mathsf{T}
such that all the objects of
\mathsf{T}
are
direct limits of objects from
\mathsf{S}

[
1
, Definition 1.9]
.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal.
Then the abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is locally finitely
presentable.
An
I
-torsion
R
-module
M
is finitely presented as an object
of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
if and only if it is finitely presented as
an object of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Equivalently,
M
is finitely presented in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
if and
only if there exists an integer
n\geq 1
such that
I^{n}M=0

and

the
R/I^{n}
-module
M
is finitely presented.

Proof.

Clearly, any finitely presented object of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
must be
finitely generated as an
R
-module; hence there exists
n\geq 1

such that
I^{n}M=0
.
If this is the case, then Lemma
9.1
(a)
says that
M
is finitely presented over
R/I^{n}
if and only if it is
finitely presented over
R
.
Any object
M\in R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
that is finitely presented in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is also finitely presented in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, since
the full subcategory
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is closed under direct limits
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
Similarly, any object of
R/I^{n}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
that is finitely presented in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is also finitely presented in
R/I^{n}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, since
the full subcategory
R/I^{n}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
is closed under direct limits in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
This proves the second and third assertions of the lemma.
It follows that representatives of isomorphism classes of finitely
presented objects form a set of generators in the abelian category

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, hence the category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is locally
finitely presentable by
[
1
, Theorem 1.11]
.
(Notice that, for abelian categories, there is no difference between
a set of generators and a set of strong generators.)
∎

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal.
We will say that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent
if
the rings
R/I^{n}
are coherent for all integers
n\geq 1
.
Clearly, an
I
-adically coherent ring
R
need not be coherent
(take
I=R
).
Moreover, the counterexample in
Remark
9.2
shows that
coherence of the ring
R/I
does
not
imply coherence of
the ring
R/I^{2}
.
However, by Lemma
9.1
(b), any
coherent ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent with respect to every
finitely generated ideal
I\subset R
.

Corollary 9.4
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I
,
J\subset R
be two finitely
generated ideals such that
\sqrt{I}\subset\sqrt{J}
.
Assume that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Then
R
is also
J
-adically coherent.

Proof.

We have
I\subset\sqrt{I}\subset\sqrt{J}
.
Since the ideal
I
is finitely generated, it follows that there exists

m\geq 1
for which
I^{m}\subset J
.
Hence
I^{mn}\subset J^{n}
for all
n\geq 1
.
Since the ring
A=R/I^{mn}
is coherent by assumption and the ideal

J^{n}/I^{mn}\subset A
is finitely generated, it follows by virtue of
Lemma
9.1
(b) that the ring
A/J^{n}
is
coherent, too.
∎

Similarly to the definition above, given a finitely generated ideal

I\subset R
, let us say that the ring
R
is
I
-adically
Noetherian
if the ring
R/I
is Noetherian.
If this is the case, then all the rings
R/I^{n}
,
n\geq 1
, are
Noetherian, too (see
Remark
9.2
).
Similarly to Corollary
9.4
,
if
\sqrt{I}\subset\sqrt{J}
for finitely generated ideals
I
,

J\subset R
and
R
is
I
-adically Noetherian, then
R
is
also
J
-adically Noetherian.

Weak proregularity of a finitely generated ideal
I\subset R
does
not imply
I
-adic coherence of
R
(for example, the zero ideal in
any commutative ring is weakly proregular).
The two properties are independent of each other:
the converse implication is not true, either, as the following
remark explains.

Remark 9.5
.

All ideals in Noetherian commutative rings are weakly proregular.
However, if the ring
R
is
I
-adically Noetherian, then the ideal

I\subset R

need not
be weakly proregular.
It suffices to consider the case of a principal ideal
I=(s)\subset R
.

Given an element
s\in R
, one says that the ring
R
has
bounded

s
-torsion
if there exists an integer
n_{0}\geq 1
such that
s^{n}r=0

for
r\in R
and
n\geq 1
implies
s^{n_{0}}r=0
.
It is easy to see that the principal ideal
I=(s)
is weakly proregular
in
R
if and only if the
s
-torsion in
R
is bounded.
Now let
k
be a field,
S=k[s]
be the ring of polynomials in one
variable
s
over
k
, and
P=k[s,s^{-1}]/k[s]
be the Prüfer

S
-module.
Consider the trivial extension
R=S\oplus P
.
So
S
is a subring in
R
, the product of any two elements from
S

and
P
in
R
is given by the action of
S
on
P
, and the product
of any two elements from
P
in
R
vanishes.
Then the
s
-torsion is not bounded in
R
; hence the ideal
I=(s)
is
not weakly proregular in
R
.
However, the quotient ring
R/I
is isomorphic to
k
,
while the quotient ring
R/I^{n}
is isomorphic to
k[s]/(s^{n})

for every
n\geq 1
; all these quotient rings are Noetherian.

To give another example, consider the ring
k[s;x_{1},x_{2},\dotsc,]
as
in Remark
9.2
; and let
R

be the quotient ring of
k[s;x_{1},x_{2},\dotsc,]
by the ideal spanned by
the elements
s^{n}x_{n}
,
n\geq 1
.
Then the
s
-torsion in
R
is not bounded; so the ideal
I=(s)
is
not weakly proregular in
R
.
Still, for every
n\geq 1
, the quotient ring
R/I^{n}
is the ring of
polynomials in a countable set of variables
x_{n}
,
x_{n+1}
, … over a commutative
k
-algebra with a finite set of generators
s
,

x_{1}
, …,
x_{n-1}
.
So the ring
R/I^{n}
is coherent.

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely
generated ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Then finitely presented objects form a set of generators of

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, and the full subcategory of finitely presented
objects is closed under kernels, cokernels, and extensions
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
In other words,
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is a locally coherent Grothendieck
category in the sense of
[
51
, Section 2]
,

[
49
, Section 8.2]
.

Proof.

Follows from Lemma
9.3

together with the fact that the full subcategory of finitely
presented
R/I^{n}
-modules is closed under kernels, cokernels, and
extensions in
R/I^{n}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
for every
n\geq 1
.
∎

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
We will say that an
I
-torsion
R
-module
M
is
coherent

(as an
I
-torsion
R
-module) if
M
is finitely generated and
every finitely generated submodule of
M
is finitely presented as
a module over
R/I^{n}
for some
n\geq 1
.
In view of Lemma
9.1
(a),

M
is coherent as an
I
-torsion
R
-module if and only if it is
coherent as an
R
-module.
It follows from Lemma
9.6
that
an
I
-torsion
R
-module is coherent if and only if it is
finitely presented as an object of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

The following definition is most useful in the
I
-adically coherent
case, but makes sense for any finitely generated ideal
I
in
a commutative ring
R
.
An
I
-torsion
R
-module
K
is said to be
fp-injective

(as an
I
-torsion
R
-module) if
\Ext^{1}_{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}}(M,K)=0

for all finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-modules
M
.
Clearly, all injective objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
are
fp-injective.
Denote the class of fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules by

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

Specializing the previous definition to the case of a ring
R

with the zero ideal
I=0
(when all
R
-modules are
I
-torsion),
we obtain the classical concept of an
fp-injective

R
-module

[
56
]
.
Notice that an fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-module
need not

be fp-injective as an
R
-module.

Lemma 9.7
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
In this context:

(a)
An
I
-torsion
R
-module
K
is fp-injective if and
only if the functor
M\longmapsto\Hom_{R}(M,K)
is exact on the abelian
category of finitely presented/conerent
I
-torsion
R
-modules
M
.

(b)
An
I
-torsion
R
-module
K
is fp-injective if and
only if
\Ext^{n}_{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}}(M,K)=0
for all finitely presented

I
-torsion
R
-modules
M
and all
n\geq 1
.

(c)
The full subcategory of fp-injective objects is closed
under extensions, cokernels of injective morphisms, infinite direct
sums, and direct limits in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

(d)
For any finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-module
M
,
the functor
\Hom_{R}(M,{-})
is exact on the exact category of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules.

(e)
An
I
-torsion
R
-module
K
is fp-injective if and
only if the
R/I^{n}
-module
\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},K)
is fp-injective
for every
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

The assertions (a–c) hold for an arbitrary locally coherent
Grothendieck category
\mathsf{A}
in place of
\mathsf{A}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
See, e. g.,
[
58
, Appendix B]
.
Part (e) follows from part (a), and part (d) follows from
the definitions (while the existence of the inherited exact structure
on the full subcategory of fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
follows from part (c)).
Another relevant reference is
[
34
, Lemma E.2.1]
.
∎

In the case of a coherent ring
R
, it is clear from
Lemma
9.7
(a) that the functor

\Gamma_{I}\colon R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
takes fp-injective

R
-modules to fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules.

If the ring
R
is
I
-adically Noetherian, then the abelian
category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is locally Noetherian.
In this case, the Noetherian objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
are simply
the
I
-torsion
R
-modules that are finitely generated as

R
-modules.
All finitely generated
I
-torsion
R
-modules are finitely
presented in this case, and all fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
are injective (as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
).

The following lemma complements
Lemma
3.7
(a).
Taken together, these two lemmas form a dual-analogous version of
Lemma
9.7
(c).

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Then the full subcategory of contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-modules
is closed under infinite products in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

Proof.

The assertion holds because the functor
P\longmapsto P/I^{n}P\colon R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow R/I^{n}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
preserves infinite products for all

n\geq 1
(as the ideal
I^{n}\subset R
is finitely generated), and
infinite products of flat
R/I^{n}
-modules are flat
R/I^{n}
-modules
(as the ring
R/I^{n}
is coherent).
∎

10.
Dualizing Complexes

We refer to Section
3
for
the definition of
contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-modules
.
The discussion of
finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-modules

can be found in Section
9
.
The following lemma is very general.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
I
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
R
,
and let
M
be a finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-module.
In this context:

(a)
For any contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-module
P
and
any
I
-torsion
R
-module
K
, the natural map

\Hom_{R}(M,K)\otimes_{R}P\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{R}(M,\>K\otimes_{R}P)

is an isomorphism.

(b)
For any injective object
H
of the category

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and any
I
-torsion
R
-module
K
, the natural map

M\otimes_{R}\Hom_{R}(K,H)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{R}(\Hom_{R}(M,K),H)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Part (a): let
m\geq 1
be an integer such that
M
is
an
R/I^{m}
-module.
For every
n\geq m
, denote by
K_{n}\subset K
the submodule of all
elements annihilated by
I^{n}
in
K
.
Then we have
K\otimes_{R}P=\varinjlim_{n\geq m}K_{n}\otimes_{R}P
, and therefore

\Hom_{R}(M,\>K\otimes_{R}P)\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Hom_{R}(M,\>K_{n}\otimes_{R}P)\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Hom_{R/I^{n}}(M,\>K_{n}\otimes_{R/I^{n}}P/I^{n}P)\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\bigl(\Hom_{R/I^{n}}(M,K_{n})\otimes_{R/I^{n}}P/I^{n}P\bigr)\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\bigl(\Hom_{R/I^{n}}(M,K_{n})\otimes_{R}P\bigr)\simeq\Hom_{R}(M,K)\otimes_{R}P

since
M
is a finitely presented object of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

P/I^{n}P
is a flat
R/I^{n}
-module.

Part (b): Following the discussion in
Section
1
, there exists an injective
R
-module

G
such that
H
is a direct summand of
\Gamma_{I}(G)
.
Notice that both
K
and
\Hom_{R}(M,K)
are
I
-torsion

R
-modules; hence we have
\Hom_{R}(K,\Gamma_{I}(G))=\Hom_{R}(K,G)

and
\Hom_{R}(\Hom_{R}(M,K),\Gamma_{I}(G))\simeq\Hom_{R}(\Hom_{R}(M,K),G)
.
It remains to recall that
M
is a finitely presented
R
-module
by Lemma
9.3
;
so the natural map

M\otimes_{R}\Hom_{R}(K,G)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Hom_{R}(\Hom_{R}(M,K),G)

is an isomorphism.
∎

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent (in the sense
of the definition in Section
9
).
The definition of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
was
also given in Section
9
.

Lemma 10.2
.

(a)
Let
P
be a contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-module
and
K
be an fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-module.
Then the tensor product
K\otimes_{R}P
is an fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-module.

(b)
Let
H
be an injective object of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

K
be an fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-module.
Then the
\Hom
module
\Hom_{R}(K,H)
is a contraflat

I
-contramodule
R
-module.

Proof.

Part (a): by Lemma
9.7
(a),
we need to prove that the functor
M\longmapsto\Hom_{R}(M,\>K\otimes_{R}P)

is exact on the abelian category of finitely presented
I
-torsion

R
-modules
M
.
By Lemma
10.1
(a), we
have
\Hom_{R}(M,\>K\otimes_{R}P)\simeq\Hom_{R}(M,K)\otimes_{R}P
.
It remains to point out that the functor
M\longmapsto\Hom_{R}(M,K)

is exact on the category of finitely presented
I
-torsion

R
-modules
M
, the
R
-module
\Hom_{R}(M,K)
is
I
-torsion for
all such
R
-modules
M
, and the functor
{-}\otimes_{R}P
is exact on
the category of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

Part (b): All
I
-torsion
R
-modules are direct limits of
finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-modules by
Lemma
9.3
.
As the class of finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-modules is closed
under kernels by Lemma
9.6
,
it follows easily that all short exact sequences of
I
-torsion

R
-modules are direct limits of short exact sequences of finitely
presented
I
-torsion
R
-modules.
Thus it suffices to prove that the functor
M\longmapsto M\otimes_{R}\Hom_{R}(K,H)
is exact on the abelian category of finitely
presented
I
-torsion
R
-modules
M
.
Alternatively, one can say that in order to prove that
the
R/I^{n}
-module
\Hom_{R}(K,H)/I^{n}\Hom_{R}(K,H)
is flat, it suffices
to show that the functor
M\longmapsto M\otimes_{R}\Hom_{R}(K,H)
is exact on
the abelian category of finitely presented
R/I^{n}
-modules
M
.

By Lemma
10.1
(a), we
have
M\otimes_{R}\Hom_{R}(K,H)\simeq\Hom_{R}(\Hom_{R}(M,K),H)
.
It remains to point out that the functor
M\longmapsto\Hom_{R}(M,K)

is exact on the category of finitely presented
I
-torsion

R
-modules
M
, the
R
-module
\Hom_{R}(M,K)
is
I
-torsion for
all such
R
-modules
M
, and the functor
\Hom_{R}({-},H)
is exact on
the category of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
∎

We start with the definition of a dualizing complex of modules over
a coherent commutative ring
A
.
A complex of
A
-modules
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{A}
is said to be a
dualizing
complex
if the following three conditions are satisfied:

(i
∘
)

the complex
D_{A}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of
fp-injective
A
-modules;

(ii
∘
)

the cohomology modules of the complex
D_{A}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are finitely
presented
A
-modules;

(iii
∘
)

the homothety map
A\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathsf{D}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})}(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}[*])

is an isomorphism of graded rings.

Here the complex
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{A}
is viewed as an object of the derived
category
\mathsf{D}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Let
A
be a commutative ring and
I\subset A
be an ideal.
The derived functor

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{A}(A/I,{-})\colon\mathsf{D}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}(A/I{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})

is constructed by applying the functor
\Hom_{A}(A/I,{-})\colon\mathsf{K}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{K}(A/I{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
to homotopy injective complexes of
A
-modules.
Similarly, the derived functor

A/I\otimes_{A}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}\,\colon\mathsf{D}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\mathsf{D}(A/I{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})

is constructed by applying the functor
A/I\otimes_{A}{-}\,\colon\mathsf{K}(A{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(A/I{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
to homotopy flat complexes of
A
-modules.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a weakly proregular
finitely generated ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically
coherent.
A
dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is a pseudo-dualizing complex (according to the definition in
Section
4
) satisfying the following
additional condition:

(i)

the complex
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules.

In order to prove the results below, we will have to assume that
all fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules have finite injective
dimensions as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
This assumption holds whenever there exists an integer
m\geq 0

such that every ideal in
R/I
is generated by at most
\aleph_{m}

elements
[
34
, Lemma E.2.2(a–b)]
.

In some results, we will also have to assume that all contraflat

I
-contramodule
R
-modules have finite projective dimensions
as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
.
This assumption holds whenever all flat
R/I
-modules have finite
projective dimensions
[
34
, Lemma E.2.2(c)]
(notice that
the projective dimensions of flat
R/I^{n}
-modules do not exceed
the projective dimensions of flat
R/I
-modules, since a flat

R/I^{n}
-module
F
is projective whenever the
R/I
-module
F/IF

is projective).

The following theorem establishes a comparison between the two
preceding definitions in the case when the ring
R
is coherent
(rather than merely
I
-adically coherent).

Theorem 10.3
.

Let
R
be a coherent commutative ring and
I\subset R
be
a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal.
Let
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be finite complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules.
Assume that all fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules have finite
injective dimensions in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules;

(2)

for every integer
n\geq 1
, the complex
D_{R/I^{n}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a dualizing complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules;

(3)

for some integer
n\geq 1
, the complex
D_{R/I^{n}}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a dualizing complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules.

The proof of Theorem
10.3

occupies most of the remaining part of
Section
10
.

Proposition 10.4
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Let
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a bounded below complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules.
Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

the complex
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules;

(2)

for every integer
n\geq 1
the complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules

\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex
of fp-injective
R/I^{n}
-modules concentrated in the cohomological
degrees
\leq j
, where a fixed integer
j
does not depend on
n
;

(3)

the complex
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is cohomologically bounded and there exists
an integer
n\geq 1
for which the complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules

\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex
of fp-injective
R/I^{n}
-modules.

Proof.

The implications (1)
\Longrightarrow
(2) and
(1)
\Longrightarrow
(3) hold in view of
Lemma
9.7
(e).

(2)
\Longrightarrow
(3) It suffices to prove that
H^{i}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=0

for
i>j
.
Indeed, let
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules with

H^{i}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\neq 0
.
Pick a cocycle
c\in G^{i}
representing a nonzero cohomology class
in
H^{i}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
Then there exists an integer
n\geq 1
such that
I^{n}c=0
in
G^{i}
.
Hence there is a morphism of complexes of
R
-modules

\tilde{c}\colon R/I^{n}\longrightarrow G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}[i]
inducing a nonzero map on
the cohomology.
The morphism of complexes
\tilde{c}
represents a nonzero degree
i

cohomology class of the complex
\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.

(3)
\Longrightarrow
(1)
Let
j
be an integer such that
H^{i}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=0
for
i\geq j
and
the complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules
\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
quasi-isomorphic to a bounded below complex of fp-injective

R/I^{n}
-modules concentrated in the cohomological degrees
\leq j
.
Put
Z=\ker(G^{j}\to G^{j+1})
and
N^{t}=G^{j+t}
for all integers

t\geq 0
; so
0\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow N^{0}\longrightarrow N^{1}\longrightarrow N^{2}\longrightarrow\dotsb

is the shifted canonical truncation
(\tau_{\geq j}G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})[j]
of
the complex
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then
N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a coresolution of the
R
-module
Z
by fp-injective

I
-torsion
R
-modules
N^{t}
, and we need to prove that
the
I
-torsion
R
-module
Z
is fp-injective, too.
After this is established, we will have a finite complex of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
\dotsb\longrightarrow G^{j-2}\longrightarrow G^{j-1}\longrightarrow Z\longrightarrow 0
quasi-isomorphic to
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

For any finitely presented
R/I^{n}
-module
M
and every
t\geq 1
,
we have

H^{t}\Hom_{R}(M,N^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=H^{j+t}\Hom_{R}(M,G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\\
\simeq H^{j+t}\Hom_{R/I^{n}}(M,\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))=0,

since a quasi-isomorphism of the bounded below complex of
fp-injective
R/I^{n}
-modules
\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
with a bounded
below complex of fp-injective
R/I^{n}
-modules concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
\leq j
is preserved by the functor

\Hom_{R/I^{n}}(M,{-})
.
The latter assertion holds by
Lemma
9.7
(c–d) (applied to
the zero ideal in the coherent commutative ring
R/I^{n}
).
Thus we have
\Ext_{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}}^{t}(M,Z)=0
for all
t\geq 1
.

It remains to point out that every finitely presented
I
-torsion

R
-module
L
is a finitely iterated extension of finitely
presented
R/I
-modules in the abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Indeed, we have a short exact sequence
0\longrightarrow IL\longrightarrow L\longrightarrow L/IL\longrightarrow 0
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, and the
R/I
-module

L/IL
is finitely presented as the cokernel of a morphism from
a direct sum of a finite number of copies of
L
into
L
.
In view of the coherence assumption on
R
, the kernel
IL
of
the morphism
L\longrightarrow L/IL
is a finitely presented
I
-torsion

R
-module, too; and we can proceed by induction.
∎

The following lemma is obvious.

Lemma 10.5
.

Let
R
be a coherent commutative ring and
I\subset R
be
a finitely generated ideal.
Let
V
be an
R/I
-module.
Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(1)

the
R/I
-module
V
is finitely presented;

(2)

the
R/I
-module
\Ext_{R}^{i}(R/I,V)
is finitely presented
for every
i\geq 0
.

Proof.

The implication (2)
\Longrightarrow
(1) holds because one can take

i=0
.
The implication (1)
\Longrightarrow
(2) holds for any
R
-module

V
, not necessarily annihilated by
I
, because the
R
-module
R/I

has a resolution by finitely generated projective
R
-modules.
(It is helpful to keep Lemma
9.1
(a)
in mind.)
∎

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
R
be a coherent commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely
generated ideal.
Let
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
R
-modules.
Then the following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

the cohomology
R/I
-modules of the complex of
R/I
-modules

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I,G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are finitely presented;

(2)

for every finite complex of finitely generated projective

R
-modules
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with
I
-torsion cohomology
R
-modules,
the complex of
R
-modules
\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
quasi-isomorphic to a bounded above complex of finitely generated
projective
R
-modules.

Proof.

Notice first of all that, over a coherent ring
R
, a bounded above
complex of
R
-modules
V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded
above complex of finitely generated projective
R
-modules if and
only if the cohomology
R
-modules of
V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are finitely presented.
So condition (2) means simply that the cohomology
R
-modules of
the complex
\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are finitely presented.

(2)
\Longrightarrow
(1)
Let
\mathbf{s}=(s_{1},\dotsc,s_{m})
be a finite sequence of generators of
the ideal
I\subset R
and
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R,\mathbf{s})
be the related dual
Koszul complex from Section
1
.
Then the complex of
R/I
-modules
R/I\otimes_{R}K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has zero
differential, and the one-term complex
R/I
is a direct summand of

R/I\otimes_{R}K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Hence it suffices to show that the complex

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I\otimes_{R}K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I,\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))
has finitely presented

R/I
-modules of cohomology.

Now
V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a finite complex of
R
-modules
with finitely presented cohomology
R
-modules (by (2)).
Pick a resolution
P_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
of the
R
-module
R/I
by finitely
generated projective
R
-modules.
Then the derived category object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I,V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{D}^{+}(R/I{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
, viewed as an object of the derived category of

R
-modules, is represented by the complex
\Hom_{R}(P_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
It follows that the cohomology
R/I
-modules of the complex

\Hom_{R}(R/I,V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are finitely presented as
R
-modules.
Using Lemma
9.1
(a), we see that
these cohomology modules are also finitely presented over
R/I
.

(1)
\Longrightarrow
(2)
By
[
5
, Proposition 6.1]
or
[
52
, Proposition 6.6]

(see also
[
36
, Proposition 5.1 and proof of Lemma 5.4(a)]
),
it suffices to consider the case of the dual Koszul complex

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R,\mathbf{s})
in the role of
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, as in the previous argument.
Then all the elements of
I
act on the complex
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by
endomorphisms homotopic to zero, so the cohomology
R
-modules
of the complex
V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are annihilated by
I
.
Furthermore, it follows from (1) that the cohomology
R/I
-modules
of the complex
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I,\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))\simeq\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I,G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))
are finitely presented.
We need to prove that the cohomology
R
-modules of the complex

V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Hom_{R}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are finitely presented.

For any bounded below complex of
R
-modules
V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with
the cohomology
R
-modules annihilated by
I
, the claim is that
the cohomology
R
-modules of
V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are finitely presented whenever
the cohomology
R/I
-modules of
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I,V^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
are
finitely presented.
This is provable by increasing induction on the cohomological degree
using Lemma
10.5
and keeping
Lemma
9.1
(a) in mind.
∎

Given a finitely generated ideal
I
in a commutative ring
R
,
we denote by
\mathfrak{R}=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}R/I^{n}
the
I
-adic completion
of the ring
R
.

Lemma 10.7
.

Let
I
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
R

and
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a bounded above complex of contraflat quotseparated

I
-contramodule
R
-modules.
Then the complex
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic if and only if the complex of

R/I
-modules
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}/IF^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic.

Proof.

The “only if” implication follows from
Lemma
3.7
and its proof.
The “only if” is essentially the result
of
[
32
, Corollary 0.3]
.
Specifically, let
i
be the largest integer such that
F^{i}\neq 0
.
Then the map
F^{i-1}/IF^{i-1}\longrightarrow F^{i}/IF^{i}
is surjective
by assumption.
So, denoting by
P=H^{i}(F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
the cokernel of the differential

F^{i-1}\longrightarrow F^{i}
, we obtain a (quotseparated)
I
-contramodule

R
-module
P
such that
P=IP
.
Using the fact that
sQ=Q
implies
Q=0
for an
s
-contramodule

R
-module
Q
, and arguing by induction on the number of generators
of the ideal
I
, one proves that
P=0
.
Hence the differential
F^{i-1}\longrightarrow F^{i}
is surjective; denote its
kernel by
{}^{\prime}\!F^{i-1}
.
By Lemma
3.7
and its proof,

{}^{\prime}\!F^{i-1}
is a contraflat quotseparated
I
-contramodule

R
-module and the short sequence
0\longrightarrow R/I\otimes_{R}{}^{\prime}\!F^{i-1}\longrightarrow R/I\otimes_{R}F^{i-1}\longrightarrow R/I\otimes_{R}F^{i}\longrightarrow 0
is exact.
Replacing the complex
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with the complex
\dotsb\longrightarrow F^{i-3}\longrightarrow F^{i-2}\longrightarrow{}^{\prime}\!F^{i-1}\longrightarrow 0
and proceeding in
the same way, one proves the desired assertion by decreasing induction
on the cohomological degree.
∎

Proposition 10.8
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Let
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules,
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules, and
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a morphism of complexes.
Then the homothety map
\mathfrak{R}\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
a quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
R
-modules if and only if
the homothety map

R/I\longrightarrow\Hom_{R/I}(\Hom_{R}(R/I,G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),\Hom_{R}(R/I,H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))

is a quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
R/I
-modules.

Proof.

By Lemmas
1.1
(b)
and
10.2
(b),

\Hom_{R}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a finite complex of contraflat quotseparated

I
-contramodule
R
-modules.
So is the one-term complex
\mathfrak{R}
.
Furthermore, by
Lemma
10.1
(b) we have

\Hom_{R/I}(\Hom_{R}(R/I,G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),\Hom_{R}(R/I,H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))\simeq R/I\otimes_{R}\Hom_{R}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
It remains to apply the result of
Lemma
10.7
to the cone of
the morphism of complexes
\mathfrak{R}\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem
10.3
.

The coherence assumption on the ring
R
obviously implies
the
I
-adic coherence.
For any weakly proregular finitely generated ideal
I
in
a commutative ring
R
such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent,
and for any bounded below complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, the complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules

\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
represents the derived category object

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.

Indeed, following the definition above, in order to compute the derived
category object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{D}^{+}(R/I^{n}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
, one
needs to apply the functor
\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},{-})
to a bounded below
complex of injective
R
-modules
J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
quasi-isomorphic to
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Let
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a bounded below complex of injective objects in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
quasi-isomorphic to
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
as a complex of

I
-torsion
R
-modules.
Then we have quasi-isomorphisms of complexes of
R
-modules

G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
By Lemma
3.6
(a), the induced map
of complexes of
R/I^{n}
-modules
\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},J^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a quasi-isomorphism.
It follows from
Lemma
9.7
(c–d) that
the induced map of complexes of
R/I^{n}
-modules
\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a quasi-isomorphism, too.

Now let
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a bounded below complex of fp-injective

I
-torsion
R
-modules quasi-isomorphic to
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then it is clear from
Proposition
10.4
(1)
\Leftrightarrow
(3) that the complex
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules if and only if the complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex
of fp-injective
R/I^{n}
-modules for some (or equivalently, for all)

n\geq 1
.
So condition (i) holds for
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
if and only if
condition (i
∘
) holds for
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.

Now assume that condition (i) is satisfied for
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Let
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules quasi-isomorphic to
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Applying Proposition
10.6

(and replacing
I
by
I^{n}
if needed), we see that the complex
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

satisfies condition (ii) from Section
4

if and only if the complex of
R/I^{n}
-modules
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

satisfies condition (ii
∘
) for some (or equivalently, for all)

n\geq 1
.

Finally, in order to compare conditions (iii) and (iii
∘
),
we need the assumption that all fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules have finite injective dimensions.
Let
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
endowed with a quasi-isomorphism
G^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then Proposition
10.8

(for the ideal
I^{n}
in the role of
I
) tells us that the complex

D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
satisfies condition (iii) from
Section
4
if and only if the complex
of
R/I^{n}
-modules
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
satisfies
condition (iii
∘
) for some (or equivalently, for all)
n\geq 1
.
∎

Example 10.9
.

Let
R
be a coherent commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a weakly
proregular finitely generated ideal.
Let
D_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dualizing complex of
R
-modules, and let

G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of fp-injective
R
-modules
quasi-isomorphic to
D_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, as per condition (i
∘
).

Then
D_{R/I}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=G_{R/I}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\Hom_{R}(R/I,G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a finite complex
of fp-injective
R/I
-modules.
Condition (ii
∘
) is satisfied for
D_{R/I}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, since
the complex
D_{R/I}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
represents the derived category

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I,D_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{D}^{+}(R/I{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
, which, viewed as an object
of the derived category of
R
-modules, can be also computed using
a resolution of
R/I
by finitely generated projective
R
-modules.
Lemma
9.1
(a) is helpful here.

Assuming further that
D_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
all fp-injective
R
-modules have
finite injective dimensions, condition (iii
∘
) is also
satisfied for
D_{R/I}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Indeed, let
H_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of injective
R
-modules
endowed with a quasi-isomorphism
G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow H_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then
R\longrightarrow\Hom_{R}(G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a quasi-isomorphism of
finite complexes of flat
R
-modules (by
Lemma
10.2
(b) applied to
the zero ideal in
R
), so it remains a quasi-isomorphism after
the functor
R/I\otimes_{R}{-}
is applied.
It remains to point out the natural isomorphisms of complexes of

R/I
-modules
R/I\otimes_{R}\Hom_{R}(G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq\Hom_{R/I}(\Hom_{R}(R/I,G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),H_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq\Hom_{R/I}(\Hom_{R}(R/I,G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),\Hom_{R}(R/I,H_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))
.

Now consider the finite complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules

D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=\mathbb{R}\Gamma_{I}(D_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=\Gamma_{I}(G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
.
By
[
53
, Theorem 3.2(iii)]
,
[
31
, Corollary 4.26]
,
or
[
36
, Lemma 1.2(a)]
, the complex
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic
to the tensor product
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(R,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}D_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, where
\mathbf{s}
is
any finite sequence of generators of the ideal
I\subset R
and

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(R,\mathbf{s})
is the infinite dual Koszul complex from
Section
1
.
We claim that
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a dualizing complex of
I
-torsion

R
-modules, because it satisfies the condition of
Theorem
10.3
(2).
In fact,
\Gamma_{I}(G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a finite complex of fp-injective

I
-torsion
R
-modules as per the paragraph after
Lemma
9.7
, so one has

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},\Gamma_{I}(G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))=\Hom_{R}(R/I^{n},G_{R}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})=D_{R/I}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in view of the argument in
the beginning of the proof of
Theorem
10.3
.

11.
Co-Contra Correspondence for a Dualizing Complex

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a weakly proregular
finitely generated ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically
coherent, and let
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dualizing complex of
I
-torsion

R
-modules.
Let us choose the parameter
l_{2}
in such a way that
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
quasi-isomorphic to a complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-d_{1}\leq m\leq l_{2}
.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring and
I\subset R
be a weakly proregular
finitely generated ideal such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically
coherent.
Let
n\geq 0
be an integer such that the injective dimensions of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules (as objects of

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) do not exceed
n
.

Let
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules,
and let the parameter
l_{2}
be chosen as stated above.
Then the related minimal corresponding classes

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}=\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
and
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}=\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})

(defined in Section
6
) are contained
in the classes of fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
and contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-modules,

\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
and

\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
.

Moreover, the classes
\mathsf{E}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
and

\mathsf{F}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
satisfy conditions (I–IV)
from Section
5
with the parameters

l_{1}=n+d_{1}
and
l_{2}
.

Proof.

In view of Remark
6.2
, it suffices to prove
the moreover clause.
Indeed, condition (I) holds for
\mathsf{E}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
by
Lemma
9.7
(c), and condition (II)
holds for
\mathsf{F}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
by
Lemma
3.7
(a) and the paragraph
preceding it.

To prove condition (III), let
E\in\mathsf{E}
be an fp-injective

I
-torsion
R
-module.
By assumption, there exists a finite injective coresolution

0\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow H^{0}\longrightarrow H^{1}\longrightarrow\dotsb\longrightarrow H^{n}\longrightarrow 0

of
E
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Let
{}^{\prime}\!D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules concentrated in the cohomological degrees between

-d_{1}
and
l_{2}
and quasi-isomorphic to
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
By Lemma
3.6
(a), the complex
of
R
-modules
\Hom_{R}({}^{\prime}\!D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
represents the derived
category object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\in\mathsf{D}^{+}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
Clearly, the complex
\Hom_{R}({}^{\prime}\!D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is concentrated
in the cohomological degrees from
-l_{2}
to
n+d_{1}
.
By Lemma
10.2
(b),

\Hom_{R}({}^{\prime}\!D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a complex of contraflat

I
-contramodule
R
-modules.

To prove condition (IV), let
F\in\mathsf{F}
be a contraflat

I
-contramodule
R
-module.
By Corollary
3.8
,
the complex of
R
-modules
{}^{\prime}\!D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}F
represents the derived
category object
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{-}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
Clearly, the complex
{}^{\prime}\!D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}F
is concentrated in
the cohomological degrees from
-d_{1}
to
l_{2}
.
By Lemma
10.2
(a),

{}^{\prime}\!D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}F
is a complex of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules.
∎

Proposition 11.2
.

Let
I
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
R
such
that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Then

(a)
For any coderived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{co}
or
\mathsf{bco}
,
the inclusion of exact categories
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
induces a triangulated equivalence of the coderived
categories

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}).

(b)
A complex in the exact category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}

is Becker-coacyclic if and only if it is acyclic, that is

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}})=\mathsf{Ac}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}})
.
So, for the exact category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
, the Becker
coderived category coincides with the derived category,

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}})=\mathsf{D}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}).

(c)
If all fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules have finite
injective dimensions, then the classes of Positselski-coacyclic and
Becker-coacyclic complexes in the abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

coincide, that is

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
.
The inclusion of additive/abelian categories
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
induces a triangulated equivalence between
the homotopy category and the coderived category

\mathsf{K}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})

in this case.

Proof.

Part (a): the case of the Positselski coderived category,
\star=\mathsf{co}
,
is an application of the dual version
of
[
34
, Proposition A.3.1(b)]
.
(Notice that the full subcategory
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
is
closed under infinite direct sums in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
by
Lemma
9.7
(c).)
The case of the Becker coderived category,
\star=\mathsf{bco}
, follows from
Theorem
2.2
(a)
(for
\mathsf{A}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) together with the fact that the classes of
injective objects in the abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and in
the exact category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
coincide.

Part (b) is a particular case (for
\mathsf{A}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) of a result
applicable to all locally coherent Grothendieck categories
\mathsf{A}
;
see
[
58
, Proposition 6.11]
.
One only needs to point out that the classes of injective objects
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
and in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
coincide
(as
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
is a coresolving subcategory closed
under direct summands in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, by
Lemma
9.7
(c)); so a complex
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
is Becker-coacyclic in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
if and only if it is Becker-coacyclic
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Part (c) is a particular case of
[
34
, Theorem B.7.7(a)]
.
∎

Proposition 11.3
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Then

(a)
For any contraderived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{ctr}

or
\mathsf{bctr}
, the inclusion of exact categories
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
induces a triangulated equivalence of
the contraderived categories

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

(b)
A complex in the exact category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}

is Becker-contraacyclic if and only if it is acyclic, that is

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}})=\mathsf{Ac}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}})
.
So, for the exact category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
, the Becker
contraderived category coincides with the derived category,

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}})=\mathsf{D}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}).

(c)
If all contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-modules have
finite projective dimensions, then the classes of
Positselski-contraacyclic and Becker-contraacyclic complexes in
the abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
coincide, that is

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
The inclusion of additive/abelian categories
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
induces a triangulated equivalence between
the homotopy category and the contraderived category

\mathsf{K}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

in this case.

Proof.

The assumption of weak proregularity of the ideal
I
is only used
in parts (a–b), and only in order to claim that all
I
-contramodule

R
-modules are quotseparated,
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

(cf.
[
42
, Corollary 3.7 and Remark 3.8]
).
Without the weak proregularity assumption, part (c) holds
in both the contexts of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
and
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
,
while parts (a) and (b) hold for
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
.

Part (a): the case of the Positselski contraderived category,

\star=\mathsf{ctr}
, is an application of
[
34
, Proposition A.3.1(b)]
.
(Notice that the full subcategory
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
is
closed under infinite products in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
by
Lemma
9.8
.)
The case of the Becker contraderived category,
\star=\mathsf{bctr}
, follows
from Theorem
2.2
(b)
(for
\mathsf{B}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
) together with the fact that the classes of
projective objects in the abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
and in
the exact category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
coincide.

Part (b) is a particular case of
[
46
, Theorems 5.1 and 6.1]
,
which are applicable in view of
[
42
, Proposition 1.5]
.
One only needs to point out that the classes of projective objects
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
and in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
coincide
(as
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
is a resolving subcategory closed
under direct summands in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
, by
Lemma
3.7
(a)); so a complex
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
is Becker-contraacyclic in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
if and only if it is Becker-coacyclic
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
Part (c) is a particular case of
[
34
, Theorem B.7.7(b)]
.
∎

Corollary 11.4
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Assume that the injective dimensions of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules (as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) are finite.
Let
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules.
Then there is a triangulated equivalence between the Becker coderived
and contraderived categories

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
11.1
,
the pair of classes
\mathsf{E}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
and

\mathsf{F}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
satisfies conditions (I–IV)
from Section
5
with suitable parameters
l_{1}

and
l_{2}
.
By Proposition
11.2
(a–b),
we have
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}})
.
By
Proposition
11.3
(a–b),
we have
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}})
.
Now the desired triangulated equivalence
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})\simeq\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})

is provided by Theorem
5.2
.
∎

Corollary 11.5
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Assume that the injective dimensions of fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules (as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) are finite, and
the projective dimensions of contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-modules
(as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
) are finite.
Let
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules.
Then there is a triangualted equivalence between the coderived and
contraderived categories

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}=\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}=\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors
