\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.
Here the notation
\mathsf{co}=\mathsf{bco}
and
\mathsf{ctr}=\mathsf{bctr}
means that
the Positselski co/contraderived categories coincide with
the Becker ones in this case.

Furthermore, there is a chain of triangulated equivalences

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}=\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}=\varnothing}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}=\varnothing}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})\simeq\mathsf{K}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}})\\
\simeq\mathsf{K}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}=\varnothing}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}=\varnothing}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}=\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Here the notation
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{abs}=\varnothing}(\mathsf{T})
means that
the absolute derived category concides with the conventional derived
category for an exact category
\mathsf{T}
.
Moreover, for any conventional derived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,

+
,
-
, or
\varnothing
, there are triangulated equivalences

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})\simeq\mathsf{K}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{inj}})\\
\simeq\mathsf{K}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{proj}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}).

Proof.

Under the assumptions of the corollary, one has

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
by
Proposition
11.2
(c)
and
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
by
Proposition
11.3
(c).
So the first assertion follows from
Corollary
11.4
.

The rest of the proof is very similar to that
of
[
39
, Corollary 7.6]
.
The exact categories
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
and

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
have finite homological dimensions
by assumption.
Hence so do their full subcategories
\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}}
and
\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}}

satisfying condition (I) or (II).
It follows easily (see, e. g.,
[
33
, Remark 2.1]

or
[
34
, Theorem B.7.6]
) that a complex in any one of these
exact categories is acyclic if and only if it is absolutely acyclic,
and that their conventional/absolute derived categories are
equivalent to the homotopy categories of complexes of injective
or projective objects.
The same applies to the Becker coderived/contraderived categories,
and also to the Positselski/coderived/contraderived categories of
those of these exact categories that happen to be closed under
infinite direct sums/products in their respective abelian categories
of torsion modules/contramodules.
The same applies also to the bounded versions of the derived
and homotopy categories.

Propositions
11.2
(a,c)
and
11.3
(a,c)
provide the equivalences of the categories mentioned in the previous
paragraph with the coderived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}=\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})

or the contraderived category
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}=\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
The equivalence
\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{E}^{l_{2}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{F}^{l_{2}})
can be
obtained as a particular case of
Theorem
6.5
.
∎

12.
Quotflat Morphisms of Ring-Ideal Pairs

Let
I
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
R
and

J
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
S
.
Suppose that we are given a ring homomorphism
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
such
that
f(I)\subset J
.
The aim of this section is to discuss a flatness condition on
a ring homomorphism
f
depending on the ideals
I
and
J
.
We proceed to deduce applications to the preservation of
fp-injectivity (of torsion modules) and contraflatness
(of contramodules) by the restriction of scalars.

Lemma 12.1
.

In the setting above, the following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

there exist descending sequences of finitely generated ideals

\dotsb\subset I_{n+1}\subset I_{n}\subset\dotsb\subset R
and

\dotsb\subset J_{n+1}\subset J_{n}\subset\dotsb\subset S
, indexed by
the integers
n\geq 1
, such that

•

one has
I_{n}\subset I^{n}
and
J_{n}\subset J^{n}
for every
n\geq 1
;

•

for every
n\geq 1
there exists
q\geq n
such that

I^{q}\subset I_{n}
and
J^{q}\subset J_{n}
;

•

one has
f(I_{n})\subset J_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
, and the ring

S/J_{n}
is a flat module over the ring
R/I_{n}
.

(2)

for every pair of integers
p\geq m\geq 1
and every finitely
generated ideal
I^{\prime}\subset R
such that
I^{p}\subset I^{\prime}\subset I^{m}
,
there exists an integer
q\geq m
and a finitely generated ideal

J^{\prime}\subset S
such that
J^{q}\subset J^{\prime}\subset J^{m}
, and the following
conditions hold:

•

one has
f(I^{\prime})\subset J^{\prime}
, and the ring
S/J^{\prime}
is a flat module
over the ring
R/I^{\prime}
.

Proof.

(1)
\Longrightarrow
(2)
By (1), we have
f(I_{p})\subset J_{p}\subset J^{p}
, and the ring
S/J_{p}

is a flat module over
R/I_{p}
.
Put
J^{\prime}=SI^{\prime}+J_{p}
(where
SI^{\prime}
denotes the ideal generated by
f(I^{\prime})

in
S
).
Then there exists
q\geq p
such that
J^{q}\subset J_{p}
, hence

J^{q}\subset J^{\prime}
.
Furthermore,
J^{\prime}\subset SI^{m}+J^{p}\subset J^{m}
.
Finally, we have
S/J^{\prime}=R/I^{\prime}\otimes_{R/I_{p}}S/J_{p}
, so flatness of

S/J_{p}
as a module over
R/I_{p}
implies flatness of
S/J^{\prime}
as
a module over
R/I^{\prime}
.

(2)
\Longrightarrow
(1)
The construction of ideals
I_{n}\subset R
and
J_{n}\subset S

proceeds by induction on
n\geq 1
.
For
n=1
, put
p=m=1
and
I^{\prime}=I
.
By (2), there exists an integer
q\geq 1
and a finitely generated ideal

J^{\prime}\subset S
such that
J^{q}\subset J^{\prime}\subset J
,
f(I)\subset J^{\prime}
,
and the ring
S/J^{\prime}
is a flat module over
R/I
.
So we can put
I_{1}=I
and
J_{1}=J^{\prime}
.

Suppose that we already have ideals
I_{n}\subset R
and
J_{n}\subset S

such that
I^{q}\subset I_{n}
and
J^{q}\subset J_{n}
for some
q\geq n
,
and the other listed conditions in (1) are satisfied.
Put
p=m=q+1
and
I^{\prime}=I^{q+1}
.
By (2), there exists an integer
q^{\prime}\geq m
and a finitely generated
ideal
J^{\prime}\subset S
such that
J^{q^{\prime}}\subset J^{\prime}\subset J^{q+1}
,

f(I^{q+1})\subset J^{\prime}
, and the ring
S/J^{\prime}
is a flat module
over
R/I^{q+1}
.
So we can put
I_{n+1}=I^{q+1}
and
J_{n+1}=J^{\prime}
, and have

I^{q^{\prime}}\subset I_{n+1}\subset I^{n+1}
,
I_{n+1}\subset I_{n}

and
J^{q^{\prime}}\subset J_{n+1}\subset J^{n+1}
,
J_{n+1}\subset J_{n}
.
∎

We will speak of the
morphism of pairs

f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
, meaning that
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
is a ring homomorphism,

I\subset R
and
J\subset S
are finitely generated ideals,
and
f(I)\subset J
.
We say that a morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is

quotflat
if the equivalent conditions of
Lemma
12.1
are satisfied.

Examples 12.2
.

(0) Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such
that
S
is a flat
R
-module, and let
I\subset R
be a finitely
generated ideal.
Let
J=SI
be the ideal generated by
f(I)
in
S
.
Then the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is quotflat.
Indeed, put
I_{n}=I^{n}
and
J_{n}=J^{n}=SI^{n}
for all
n\geq 1
.
Then the ring
S/J^{n}=R/I^{n}\otimes_{R}S
is a flat module over
R/I^{n}
for
every
n\geq 1
, so the conditions of
Lemma
12.1
(1) are satisfied.

(1) Let
k
be a field,
R=k[x]
be the ring of polynomials in
over variable
x
over
k
, and
S=k[x,y]
be the ring of polynomials
in two variables
x
,
y
.
Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be the natural inclusion map.
Consider the maximal ideal
I=(x)\subset R
and the maximal ideal

J=(x,y)\subset R
.
Then the ring
S/J^{n}
is
not
a flat module over
R/I^{n}
when

n\geq 2
.
For example, the ring
S/J^{2}
is not a flat module over
R/I^{2}
,
since the coset
y+J^{2}\in S/J^{2}
is annihilated by multliplication
with the coset
x+I^{2}\in R/I^{2}
, but
y+J^{2}
is
not
divisible by

x+I^{2}
in
S/J^{2}
.

Nevertheless, the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is
quotflat.
Indeed, put
I_{n}=(x^{n})\subset R
and
J_{n}=(x^{n},y^{n})\subset S
.
Then the ring
S/J_{n}=k[x]/(x^{n})\otimes_{k}k[y]/(y^{n})
is a flat module
over the ring
R/I_{n}=k[x]/(x^{n})
.
We also have
I_{n}=I^{n}
and
J^{2n-1}\subset J_{n}\subset J^{n}
for
every
n\geq 1
, so the conditions of
Lemma
12.1
(1) are satisfied.

(2) In the notation of (1), let
S=R=k[x]
be the ring of polynomials
in one variable
x
over
k
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be the identity
map.
Put
I=0\subset R
and
J=(x)\subset S
.
Then the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is
not

quotflat.
Indeed, for any integers
q\geq n\geq 1
and any ideals
I^{\prime}\subset R

and
J^{\prime}\subset S
such that
I^{q}\subset I^{\prime}\subset I^{n}
and
J^{q}\subset J^{\prime}\subset J^{n}
, the Artinian ring
S/J^{\prime}
is
not
a flat module
over the polynomial ring
R/I^{\prime}=R
.

Let us emphasize that the ring
S
is a flat
R
-module in this
example.
Moreover, the
J
-adic completion
k[[x]]=\mathfrak{S}=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}S/J^{n}
of the ring
S
is a flat module over
the
I
-adic completion
k[x]=\mathfrak{R}=\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}R/I^{n}
of
the ring
R
, or in other words, one can say that
\mathfrak{S}
is a contraflat

I
-contramodule
R
-module.
Nevertheless, the morphism of pairs is not quotflat.

The following proposition explains what we need the notion of
a quotflat morphism for.

Proposition 12.3
.

Let
I
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
R
, let

J
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
S
, and let

f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is
quotflat and the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Then every fp-injective
J
-torsion
S
-module is also fp-injective
as an
I
-torsion
R
-module.

Proof.

Let
I_{n}\subset R
and
J_{n}\subset S
be descending sequences of
ideals as in Lemma
12.1
(1).
Let
H
be an fp-injective
J
-torsion
S
-module.
According to Lemma
9.7
(a),
we need to prove that the functor
M\longmapsto\Hom_{R}(M,H)
is exact
on the abelian category of finitely presented
I
-torsion

R
-modules
M
.

Notice that coherence of the rings
R/I^{q}
for all
q\geq 1
implies
coherence of the rings
R/I_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
by
Lemma
9.1
(b).
Any short exact sequence of finitely presented
I
-torsion

R
-modules is a short exact sequence of finitely presented

R/I_{m}
-modules for some
m\geq 1
; so it suffices to let
M
range
over the abelian category of finitely presented
R/I_{m}
-modules.
In this case, we have

\Hom_{R}(M,H)\simeq\Hom_{R}\bigl(M,\,\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq 1}\Hom_{S}(S/J_{n},H)\bigr)\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq 1}\Hom_{R}(M,\Hom_{S}(S/J_{n},H))\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Hom_{R/I_{n}}(M,\Hom_{S}(S/J_{n},H))\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Hom_{S/J_{n}}(S/J_{n}\otimes_{R/I_{n}}M,\>\Hom_{S}(S/J_{n},H))\\
\simeq\varinjlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Hom_{S}(S/J_{n}\otimes_{R/I_{n}}M,\>H),

where the second isomorphism holds by
Lemma
9.3
.
Now, for any finitely presented
R/I_{n}
-module
M
,
the
S/J_{n}
-module
S/J_{n}\otimes_{R/I_{n}}M
is finitely presented;
hence
S/J_{n}\otimes_{R/I_{n}}M
is also finitely presented as
a (
J
-torsion)
S
-module.
Since
S/J_{n}
is flat as a module over
R/I_{n}
and
H
is
fp-injective as a
J
-torsion
S
-module, it is clear that
the functor
M\longmapsto\Hom_{S}(S/J_{n}\otimes_{R/I_{n}}M,\>H)
is exact
on the category of finitely presented
R/I_{m}
-modules
M
.
∎

In the rest of this section, our aim is to prove the following
dual-analogous version of
Proposition
12.3
.

Proposition 12.4
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
, let
J
be a finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism
such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is
quotflat and the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Then every contraflat quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-module is
also contraflat as an
I
-contramodule
R
-module.

The proof of Proposition
12.4
is
based on a sequence of lemmas.

Lemma 12.5
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Let
\Xi
be an indexing set and
(P_{\xi})_{\xi\in\Xi}
be a family
of
I
-contramodule
R
-modules.
Let
M
be a finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-module.
Then the natural map

\Tor_{i}^{R}\left(M,\>\prod\nolimits_{\xi\in\Xi}P_{\xi}\right)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{\xi\in\Xi}\Tor_{i}^{R}(M,P_{\xi})

is an isomorphism for all
i\geq 0
.

Proof.

For every index
\xi\in\Xi
, choose a resolution
F_{\xi,{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\longrightarrow P_{\xi}
of the
I
-contramodule
R
-module
P_{\xi}
by contraflat

I
-contramodule
R
-modules
F_{\xi,i}
,
i\geq 0
.
(For example, any projective resolutions in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

are suitable.)
Then
\prod_{\xi\in\Xi}F_{\xi,{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}\longrightarrow\prod_{\xi\in\Xi}P_{\xi}

is a resolution of the
I
-contramodule
R
-module

\prod_{\xi\in\Xi}P_{\xi}
, and by
Lemma
9.8

the
I
-contramodule
R
-modules
\prod_{\xi\in\Xi}F_{\xi,i}

are contraflat for all
i\geq 0
.
By Corollary
3.8
,
the complex
M\otimes_{R}F_{\xi,{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}
computes
\Tor_{*}^{R}(M,P_{\xi})
for
every
\xi\in\Xi
, and the complex
M\otimes_{R}\prod_{\xi\in\Xi}F_{\xi,{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}

computes
\Tor_{*}^{R}\bigl(M,\>\prod_{\xi\in\Xi}P_{\xi}\bigr)
.
It remains to point out that
M
is a finitely presented
R
-module
by Lemma
9.3
, so
the functor
M\otimes_{R}{-}
preserves infinite products of
R
-modules.
∎

Lemma 12.6
.

Let
I
be an ideal in a commutative ring
R
, and let
M
be
an
R/I^{m}
-module for some integer
m\geq 1
.
Then the projective system of
\Tor_{1}^{R}
modules

(\Tor^{R}_{1}(M,R/I^{n}))_{n\geq 1}

is pro-zero (in the sense of the definition in
Section
1
).

Proof.

One can immediately see from the homological
\Tor_{*}^{R}
sequence
induced by the short exact sequence of
R
-modules
0\longrightarrow I^{n}\longrightarrow R\longrightarrow R/I^{n}\longrightarrow 0
that
\Tor^{R}_{1}(M,R/I^{n})
is
the kernel of the natural map
M\otimes_{R}I^{n}\longrightarrow M
.
Clearly, any projective subsystem (projective system of subobjects)
of a pro-zero projective system is pro-zero; so it suffices to check
that the projective system
(M\otimes_{R}I^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is pro-zero.
Indeed, we have
M\otimes_{R}I^{n}\simeq M\otimes_{R/I^{m}}(R/I^{m}\otimes_{R}I^{n})\simeq M\otimes_{R/I^{m}}(I^{n}/I^{n+m})
, and it remains to point out
that the map
I^{k}/I^{k+m}\longrightarrow I^{j}/I^{j+m}
vanishes for all
positive integers
j
and
k
such that
k\geq j+m
.
∎

Lemma 12.7
.

Let
I
be an ideal in a commutative ring
R
and
\dotsb\subset I_{n+1}\subset I_{n}\subset\dotsb\subset R
be a descending sequence of
ideals, indexed by the integers
n\geq 1
, such that for every
n\geq 1

there exists an integer
q\geq n
for which
I^{q}\subset I_{n}\subset I^{n}
.
Let
(G_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a projective system of
R
-modules such
that
G_{n}
is a flat
R/I_{n}
-module for every
n\geq 1
.
Let
M
be an
R/I^{m}
-module for some integer
m\geq 1
.
Then one has

\varprojlim\nolimits_{n\geq 1}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,G_{n})=0=\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n\geq 1}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,G_{n}),

where
\varprojlim^{1}_{n\geq 1}
denotes the first derived functor of
projective limit.

Proof.

The point is that for any
R
-module
N
one has

N\otimes_{R}G_{n}\simeq(N\otimes_{R}R/I_{n})\otimes_{R/I_{n}}G_{n}
, and the functor

{-}\otimes_{R/I_{n}}G_{n}
is exact.
Therefore, there is a natural isomorphism

\Tor_{i}^{R}(N,G_{n})\simeq\Tor^{i}_{R}(N,R/I_{n})\otimes_{R/I_{n}}G_{n}
for all
i\geq 0
.
Returning to the situation at hand, the projective system

(\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,R/I_{n}))_{n\geq 1}
is pro-zero by
Lemma
12.6
, and it follows that the projective
system
(\Tor^{i}_{R}(N,R/I_{n})\otimes_{R/I_{n}}G_{n})_{n\geq 1}
is pro-zero, too.
For a pro-zero projective system (of abelian groups or
R
-modules
indexed by nonnegative integers), both the underived and the first
derived projective limits vanish.
∎

Lemma 12.8
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
, let
J
be a finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism
such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is quotflat,
and let
I_{n}\subset R
and
J_{n}\subset S
be descending sequences
of ideals as in Lemma
12.1
(1).
Let
F
be a contraflat quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-module.
Then, for every finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-module
M
and
every integer
m\geq 1
, there is a natural short exact sequence
of
R
-modules

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{R}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}M\otimes_{R}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}M\otimes_{R}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

Proof.

By a very general result of
[
45
, Lemmas 8.1 and 8.3]
, which is
applicable in view of
[
42
, Proposition 1.5]
, all contraflat
quotseparated
J
-contramodule
R
-module are
I
-adically separated
(and complete), so we have
F\simeq\varprojlim_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F
.
As the transition maps
F/J_{n+1}F\longrightarrow F/J_{n}F
are surjective for
all
n\geq 1
, we have the telescope short exact sequence of
R
-modules

(14)

0\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muF\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

Applying the derived functor
\Tor_{*}^{R}(M,{-})

to (
14
), we obtain a long exact sequence
of
R
-modules

(15)

\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Tor_{1}^{R}\left(M,\>\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F\right)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\Tor_{1}^{R}\left(M,\>\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F\right)\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{R}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{R}\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{R}\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

By Lemma
12.5
, we have

\Tor_{i}^{R}\bigl(M,\>\prod_{n\geq m}F/J_{n}F)\simeq\prod_{n\geq m}\Tor_{i}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F)
for all
i\geq 0
(notice that
every
S/J_{n}
-module is a
J
-contramodule
S
-module, hence also
an
I
-contramodule
R
-module).
So the sequence (
15
) takes the form

(16)

\dotsb\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F)\\
\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5muM\otimes_{R}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}M\otimes_{R}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}M\otimes_{R}F/J_{n}F\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu0.

It remains to show that the map

(17)

\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F)\mskip 1.5mu\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow\mskip 1.5mu\prod\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F)

in the first line of (
16
)
is surjective.
In fact, we will see that (
17
)
is an isomorphism.
Indeed, the kernel and cokernel of (
17
)
are the projective limit and the first derived projective limit

\varprojlim\nolimits_{n\geq m}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F)\quad\text{and}\quad\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n\geq m}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F),

respectively.
It is important for us to show that

\varprojlim\nolimits^{1}_{n\geq m}\Tor_{1}^{R}(M,F/J_{n}F)=0
.
Here it suffices to notice that
F/J_{n}F
is a flat
S/J_{n}
-module
by the contraflatness assumption on
F
.
Since
S/J_{n}
is a flat
R/I_{n}
-module by one of the conditions in
Lemma
12.1
(1), it follows that

F/J_{n}F
is a flat
R/I_{n}
-module.
As a finitely presented
I
-torsion
R
-module,
M
is
an
R/I^{k}
-module for some
k\geq 1
.
Thus Lemma
12.7
is applicable.
∎

Proof of
Proposition
12.4
.

Let
I_{n}\subset R
and
J_{n}\subset S
be descending sequences
of ideals as in Lemma
12.1
(1), and
let
F
be a contraflat quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-module.
Similarly to the proof of
Lemma
10.2
(b), it suffices
to show that the functor
M\longmapsto M\otimes_{R}F
is exact on
the abelian category of finitely presented
R/I_{m}
-modules for
every
m\geq 1
.
We use the result of
Lemma
12.8
.
For every
n\geq m
, we have
M\otimes_{R}F/J_{n}F\simeq M\otimes_{R/I_{n}}F/J_{n}F
,
which is an exact functor of
M\in R/I_{m}{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
since
F/J_{n}F
is
a flat
R/I_{n}
-module (as explained in the proof of
Lemma
12.8
).
It remains to point out that the infinite products of
R
-modules
are exact functors, and the kernel of a surjective morphism is
an exact functor.
∎

Remarks 12.9
.

The assumptions in
Propositions
12.3

and
12.4
are sufficient for
the conclusions.
They are certainly
not
necessary.
Other sets of sufficient assumptions exist.
Let us describe two of them.

(1) Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such
that
S
is a flat
R
-module, let
I\subset R
be a finitely generated
ideal, and let
J=SI
be the ideal generated by
f(I)
in
S

(as in Example
12.2
(0)).
Then all fp-injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules are also fp-injective
as
I
-torsion
R
-modules, and all contraflat
J
-contramodule

S
-modules are contraflat as
I
-contramodule
R
-modules.

Indeed, if
J=SI
, then the functor of extension of scalars

S\otimes_{R}{-}\,\colon R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
takes
I
-torsion
R
-modules
to
J
-torsion
S
-modules.
So we have an exact functor
S\otimes_{R}{-}\,\colon R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
left adjoint to the exact functor of restriction of
scalars
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
Therefore, for any
I
-torsion
R
-module
M
and any
J
-torsion

S
-module
H
, there is a natural isomorphism of Ext modules

\Ext^{i}_{R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}}(M,H)\simeq\Ext^{i}_{S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}}(S\otimes_{R}M,\>H)
for all
i\geq 0
.
As the functor
S\otimes_{R}{-}
also takes finitely presented (
I
-torsion)

R
-modules to finitely presented (
J
-torsion)
S
-modules, it
follows that
H
is fp-injective as an
I
-torsion
R
-module
whenever it is fp-injective as a
J
-torsion
S
-module.

Dual-analogously, if
J=SI
, then one has
J^{n}=SI^{n}
for every

n\geq 1
, and it follows that
R/I^{n}\otimes_{R}F\simeq S/J^{n}\otimes_{S}F
for
any
S
-module
F
.
Since
S
is a flat
R
-module, the ring
S/J^{n}
is a flat module
over
R/I^{n}
by Example
12.2
(0).
So the
R/I^{n}
-module
F/I^{n}F
is flat whenever the
S/J^{n}
-module

F/J^{n}F
is flat.
Thus
F
is contraflat as an
I
-contramodue
R
-module whenever
it is contraflat as a
J
-contramodule
S
-module.

(2) Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a homomorphism of commutative rings such
that
S
is a flat
R
-module.
Let
I\subset R
and
J\subset S
be finitely generated ideals such
that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that the ring
S
is Noetherian.
Notice that the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)

need not
be quotflat in this case,
as Example
12.2
(2) illustrates.
Nevertheless, all fp-injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules are again
fp-injective as
I
-torsion
R
-modules, and all contraflat

J
-contramodule
S
-modules are contraflat as
I
-contramodule

R
-modules.

Indeed, if the ring
S
is
J
-adically Noetherian, then the classes
of injective and fp-injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules coincide
(see Section
9
).
If, moreover, the ring
S
is Noetherian, then a
J
-torsion

S
-module
H
is injective in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
if and only if it is
injective in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
(see
Remark
4.3
).
If this is the case, then
H
is also injective in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, since

S
is a flat
R
-module.
As
H
is
I
-torsion as an
R
-module, it follows that
H
is
injective in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

Dual-analogously, if the ring
S
is Noetherian, then
a
J
-contramodule
S
-module
F
is contraflat if and only if
F

is a flat
S
-module
[
38
, Corollary 10.3(a)]
.
If this is the case, then
F
is also a flat
R
-module, since
S

is a flat
R
-module.
As
F
is an
I
-contramodule
R
-module, it follows that
F
is
a contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-module.

In the rest of this paper, whenever the quotflatness assumption is
invoked, it is only used in order to refer to the results of
Propositions
12.3

and
12.4
.
So, if one is willing to assume that
S
is Noetherian (and flat
as an
R
-module) instead, then the quotflatness assumption
can be dropped.

13.
Relative Context and Base Change

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
and
J
be a weakly proregular finitely
generated ideal in a commutative ring
S
.
Suppose that we are given a morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
,
i. e., a ring homomorphism
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that
S
is a flat
R
-module.

Let
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a pseudo-dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
(in the sense of the definition in
Section
4
).
Let
\mathbf{s}
be a finite sequence of generators of the ideal
J\subset S
,
and let
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})
be the infinite dual Koszul complex from
Section
1
.
Recall that
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})
is a finite complex of countably
presented flat
S
-modules.

We are interested in the finite complex of
S
-modules

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Denoting by
SI\subset S
the ideal generated by
f(I)
in
S
,
we notice that both
S\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
and
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

are finite complexes of
SI
-torsion
S
-modules.
The first key observation is that
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is quasi-isomorphic to a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, which is defined uniquely up to a quasi-isomorphism of
finite complexes in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

Indeed, by
Theorem
3.4
,
the functor
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
is fully
faithful, and its essential image consists of all the bounded complexes
of
S
-modules with
J
-torsion cohomology modules.
By
[
36
, Lemma 1.1(a)]
, the cohomology
S
-modules of
the complex
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
are
J
-torsion for
any complex of
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Returning to the situation at hand, it follows that there is
a uniquely defined object
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
isomorphic
to
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.

Theorem 13.1
.

The finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
a pseudo-dualizing complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.

Proof.

As mentioned in Section
1
, the complex

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})
, viewed up to quasi-isomorphism, does not
depend on the choice of a finite sequence
\mathbf{s}
of generators of
the ideal
J\subset S
.
These are quasi-isomorphisms of finite complexes of flat
S
-modules,
so the functor
{-}\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
takes them to quasi-isomorphisms.
Thus we can choose the finite sequence
\mathbf{s}
as we prefer.
Pick a finite sequence
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},\dotsc,r_{l})
of generators of
the ideal
I\subset R
, and let
\mathbf{s}=(\mathbf{t},f(\mathbf{r}))
, where
\mathbf{t}
is some
finite sequence of elements of the ideal
J\subset S
such that
the finite sequence
\mathbf{s}
generates
J
.
Here the notation is
f(\mathbf{r})=(f(r_{1}),\dotsc,f(r_{l}))
.

Let us prove condition (ii) from
Section
4
for the complex
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Similarly to the proof of
Proposition
10.6
,
by
[
5
, Proposition 6.1]
or
[
52
, Proposition 6.6]

(see also
[
36
, Proposition 5.1 and proof of Lemma 5.4(a)]
),
it suffices to consider the case of the dual Koszul complex

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})
in the role of
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Then the complex of
S
-modules
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
quasi-isomorphic to
K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

As mentioned in the proof of
Lemma
8.1
,
following, e. g., the discussion in
[
42
, Section 2]
,
the functor
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}{-}\,\colon\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
is right adjoint to the inclusion functor

\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
, where

\mathsf{D}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
denotes the full subcategory of complexes with

J
-torsion cohomology modules in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
The complex of
S
-modules
K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})
has
J
-torsion cohomology
modules, hence the natural morphism of complexes

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\longrightarrow K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})
is
a quasi-isomorphism.
So the complex of
S
-modules
\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is
quasi-isomorphic to
K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Finally, we have an isomorphism of complexes

K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\simeq K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})\otimes_{R}K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R,\mathbf{r})
.
By assumption, condition (ii) holds for the complex of
I
-torsion

S
-modules
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, so the complex of
R
-modules

K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R,\mathbf{r})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded above
complex of finitely generated projective
R
-modules
P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
It follows that the complex of
S
-modules

\Hom_{S}(K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is quasi-isomorphic to the bounded
above complex of finitely generated projective
S
-modules

K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})\otimes_{R}P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Let us prove condition (iii) for
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
For this purpose, it is convenient to use the complexes

T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})
and
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s})
,
n\geq 1
, from
Section
1
.
We compute

(18)

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\\
\simeq\mathbb{R}\varprojlim\nolimits_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}),

where
\mathbb{R}\varprojlim_{n\geq 1}
denotes the derived functor of
projective limit of projective systems of complexes of
S
-modules
indexed by the poset of nonnegative integers.

Following Section
1
and the argument above,
the complex
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is homotopy equivalent to
the complex
K_{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}^{n})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, which is quasi-isomorphic
to a bounded above complex of finitely generated projective

S
-modules
Q_{n}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
Hence we have

(19)

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\\
=\Hom_{S}(Q_{n}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}\Hom_{S}(Q_{n}^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\\
=T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\\
\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s}),\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}))\\
\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s}),\mathfrak{R})\simeq\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s}),\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}\mathfrak{R}),

where
\mathfrak{R}=\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}R/I^{m}
.

Now we have isomorphisms of complexes of
S
-modules

T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})\otimes_{S}T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{r})\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})\otimes_{R}T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R,\mathbf{r})
.
By
[
36
, Lemma 5.3(b)]
, the completion map
R\longrightarrow\mathfrak{R}

induces a quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
R
-modules

T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R,\mathbf{r})\longrightarrow T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(R,\mathbf{r})\otimes_{R}\mathfrak{R}
.
Hence the same completion map also induces a quasi-isomorphism of
complexes of
S
-modules
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\longrightarrow T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}\mathfrak{R}
.
So, using (
19
), we can
finish the computation (
18
) as

(20)

\mathbb{R}\varprojlim\nolimits_{n\geq 1}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\\
\simeq\mathbb{R}\varprojlim\nolimits_{n\geq 1}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{n}(S,\mathbf{s}),T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}))=\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})).

Finally, the completion map
S\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}
induces a quasi-isomorphism
of complexes of
S
-modules
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\longrightarrow T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}\mathfrak{S}

by
[
36
, Lemma 5.3(b)]
, and it follows that the homothety morphism

\mathfrak{S}\longrightarrow\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}))
is an isomorphism in

\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
by
[
36
, Lemma 5.2(b)]
.
∎

Assume that the complex
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is concentrated in the cohomological
degrees
-d_{1}\leq m\leq d_{2}
and the complex
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-t_{1}\leq m\leq t_{2}
(where
d_{1}
,
d_{2}
,
t_{1}
,

t_{2}
are some integers).
The definitions of the Bass and Auslander classes
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}
and

\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}
can be found in Section
4
.

The following proposition is our version
of
[
39
, Proposition 8.5]
.
Notice the difference between their formulation, however:
The assertions of
[
39
, Proposition 8.5]
are “if and only if”
results, while the assertions of our proposition are only implications
in one direction.

Proposition 13.2
.

Let
l_{1}
be an integer such that
l_{1}\geq d_{1}
and
l_{1}\geq t_{1}
.
Then

(a)
a
J
-torsion
S
-module belongs to the full
subcategory
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
whenever
its underlying
I
-torsion
R
-module belongs to the full
subcategory
\mathsf{E}_{l_{1}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
;

(b)
a
J
-contramodule
S
-module belongs to the full
subcategory
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
whenever
its underlying
I
-contramodule
R
-module belongs to the full
subcategory
\mathsf{F}_{l_{1}}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

Proof.

Part (a): for any
S
-module
E
, we have

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\simeq\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)).

Now if
H^{n}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)=0
for
n>l_{1}
, then

H^{n}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)=0
for
n>l_{1}
(since the finite complex
of countably generated projective
S
-modules
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})
is
concentrated in the nonnegative cohomological degrees).

Similarly, one computes

U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\simeq L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E))\\
\simeq L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)),

where the second isomorphism holds because the natural map

T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a quasi-isomorphism
for any complex of
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

by
[
36
, proof of Lemma 5.2(a)]
.

Now if
E
is a
J
-torsion
S
-module and the adjunction morphism

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\longrightarrow E
is an isomorphism in

\mathsf{D}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
(hence also in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
), then
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)
is a complex of
S
-modules with
J
-torsion
cohomology modules.
Hence the natural map
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E))\longrightarrow L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)
is a quasi-isomorphism of
complexes of
S
-modules by
[
36
, Lemma 1.1(c)]
, and it follows
that the adjunction morphism
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\allowbreak\longrightarrow E
is an isomorphism whenever the adjunction morphism

L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\allowbreak\longrightarrow E

is an isomorphism.

Part (b): for any
S
-module
F
, we have

U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F).

Now if
H^{-n}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F)=0
for
n>l_{1}
, then

H^{-n}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F)=0
for
n>l_{1}
.

Similarly, one computes

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F)\simeq\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}\bigl(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>\Hom_{S}\bigl(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F)\bigr)\bigr)\\
\simeq\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F))\simeq\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),\>\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F)),

where the second isomorphism holds because the natural map

\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),\allowbreak\>T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{S}M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\longrightarrow\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is a quasi-isomorphism for any
complex of
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
by
[
36
, proof of Lemma 5.2(b)]
.

Now if
F
is a
J
-contramodule
S
-module and the adjunction
morphism
F\allowbreak\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F)
is
an isomorphism in
\mathsf{D}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
(hence also in
\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
), then

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F)
is a complex of
S
-modules
with
J
-contramodule cohomology modules.
Hence the natural map
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F)\longrightarrow\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{s}),\>\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F))

is a quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
S
-modules
by
[
36
, Lemma 2.2(c)]
, and it follows that the adjunction
morphism
F\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F)
is
an isomorphism whenever the adjunction morphism
F\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},\>L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F)
is an isomorphism.
∎

The proposition above pertains to the Bass and Auslander classes.
Now we turn to abstract corresponding classes.
Given a class of
I
-torsion
R
-modules

\mathsf{E}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, we denote by
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
the class of all
J
-torsion
S
-modules whose
underlying
I
-torsion
R
-modules belong to
\mathsf{E}
.
Similarly, given a class of
I
-contramodule
R
-modules

\mathsf{F}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
, we denote by
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
the class of all
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
whose underlying
I
-contramodule
R
-modules belong to
\mathsf{F}
.

As a special case of the setting described in the beginning of
this Section
13
, one can consider the situation
when
J=SI
is the ideal generated by
f(I)
in
S
.
Notice that weak proregularity of
I
in
R
and flatness of
S
over

R
imply weak proregularity of
J
in
S
in this
case
[
36
, Example 2.4]
.
Furthermore, an
S
-module is
SI
-torsion if and only if it is

I
-torsion as an
R
-module, and an
S
-module is
an
SI
-contramodule if and only if it is
I
-contramodule as
an
R
-module.

For an ideal
J\subset S
such that
f(I)\subset J
, one has

SI\subset J
.
Hence the exact inclusions of abelian categories
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

In this context, we will use the notation
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
for the class of all (
SI
-torsion)
S
-modules
whose underlying (
I
-torsion)
R
-modules belong to
\mathsf{E}
.
Similarly, we denote by
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

the class of all (
SI
-contramodule)
S
-modules whose underlying
(
I
-contramodule)
R
-modules belong to
\mathsf{F}
.
So we have
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\cap\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\cap\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

Proposition 13.3
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
and
J
be a weakly proregular finitely
generated ideal in the commutative ring
S
.
Assume that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that
S
is a flat
R
-module and the morphism of pairs

f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is quotflat in the sense of
Section
12
.
Let
t\geq 0
be an integer such that the ideal
J/SI
in the ring

S/SI
can be generated by
t
elements.
Let
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a pseudo-dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-d_{1}\leq m\leq d_{2}
,
and let
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
be two integers such that the complex of

S
-modules
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to
a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
concentrated
in the cohomological degrees
-t_{1}\leq m\leq t_{2}
(as per the discussion
in the beginning of this Section
13
).

Let
\mathsf{E}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{F}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

be a pair of full subcategories satisfying conditions (I–IV) from
Section
5
with respect to the pseudo-dualizing
complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with some parameters

l_{1}\geq d_{1}
and
l_{2}\geq d_{2}
.
Assume that the class
\mathsf{E}
is closed under countable direct sums in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and contains all the fp-injective
I
-torsion

R
-modules, while the class
\mathsf{F}
is closed under countable products
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
and contains all the contraflat
I
-contramodule

R
-modules.
Let
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
be two integers such that
u_{1}\geq l_{1}
,

u_{1}\geq t_{1}
and
u_{2}\geq l_{2}+t
,
u_{2}\geq t_{2}
.
Then the pair of full subcategories
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

and
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
satisfies conditions (I–IV)
with respect to the pseudo-dualizing complex of
J
-torsion

S
-modules
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with the parameters
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
12.3
, all
the injective objects of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
belong to
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
.
By Proposition
12.4
, all
the projective objects of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
belong to
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}
.
It follows that conditions (I) and (II) are satisfied for
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}

and
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}
.

Now let us choose a finite sequence of generators
\mathbf{s}
for
the ideal
J\subset S
as described in the first paragraph of
the proof of Theorem
13.1
.
By assumption, it can be done in such a way that the sequence
\mathbf{t}

consists of
t
elements.
Then we have
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})=K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,t)\otimes_{R}K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(R,\mathbf{r})
.
By
[
36
, Lemma 1.1(c)]
, the complex of
R
-modules

K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(R,\mathbf{r})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is quasi-isomorphic to
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, so
the complex of
S
-modules
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{t})\otimes_{R}L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is
quasi-isomorphic to
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Hence, similarly to the beginning of the proof of
Proposition
13.2
(a), for any

S
-module
E
we have

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\simeq\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t}),\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)).

Assume that
E\in\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
(or more generally,
E\in\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}
).
By condition (III) for the classes
\mathsf{E}
and
\mathsf{F}
with respect to
the pseudo-dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
the derived category object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
can be represented by
a complex of
I
-contramodule
R
-modules concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-l_{2}\leq m\leq l_{1}
with the terms
belonging to
\mathsf{F}
.

The full subcategories
\mathsf{F}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

and
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
are resolving (by condition (II),
which we have already proved).
In particular, so is the full subcategory
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
(cf. Remark
12.9
(1)).
In view of
[
34
, Corollary A.5.2]
, it follows that the derived
category object
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{R}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
can be
represented by a complex of
SI
-contramodule
S
-modules
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-l_{2}\leq m\leq l_{1}
with
the terms belonging to
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}
.

As
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})
is a complex of countably generated free
S
-modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
0\leq m\leq t
, and the full
subcategory
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}
is closed under countable products in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, we can conclude that the complex

\Hom_{S}(T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t}),F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
has the terms belonging to
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}
,
is concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-l_{2}-t\leq m\leq l_{1}
,
and represents the derived category object

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\in\mathsf{D}^{+}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
On the other hand, we actually have
\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
by
Lemma
3.3
(c), since

U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.

Recall that we have
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\cap\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
By
[
34
, Corollary A.5.5]
, it follows that the object

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},E)\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
can be represented
by a complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-l_{2}-t\leq m\leq l_{1}
with the terms
belonging to
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}
.
This proves condition (III) for the classes
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
and
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}

with respect to the pseudo-dualizing complex of
J
-torsion

S
-modules
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.

Dual-analogously, as in the beginning of the proof of
Proposition
13.2
(b), for any

S
-module
F
we have

U\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F\simeq T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})\otimes_{S}(L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F).

Assume that
F\in\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}
(or more generally,
F\in\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}^{\circ}
).
By condition (IV) for the classes
\mathsf{E}
and
\mathsf{F}
with respect to
the pseudo-dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
the derived category object
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
can be represented by
a complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules concentrated in the cohomological
degrees
-l_{1}\leq m\leq l_{2}
with the terms belonging to
\mathsf{E}
.

The full subcategories
\mathsf{E}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and

\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
are coresolving (by condition (I),
which we have already proved).
In particular, so is the full subcategory
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
(cf. Remark
12.9
(1)).
In view of the dual version of
[
34
, Corollary A.5.2]
, it
follows that the derived category object
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{R}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
can be represented by a complex of

SI
-torsion
S
-modules
E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
concentrated in the cohomological
degrees
-l_{1}\leq m\leq l_{2}
with the terms belonging to
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}
.

As
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})
is a complex of countably generated free
S
-modules
concentrated in the cohomological degrees
0\leq m\leq t
, and the full
subcategory
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}
is closed under countable direct sums in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
, we can conclude that the complex
T^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}(S,\mathbf{t})\otimes_{R}E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
has
the terms belonging to
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}
,
is concentrated in the cohomological degrees
-l_{1}\leq m\leq l_{2}+t
,
and represents the derived category object

U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{-}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
On the other hand, we actually have
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
by
Lemma
3.3
(a), since

U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules.

Recall that we have
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\cap\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}^{\circ}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{SI{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
By the dual version of
[
34
, Corollary A.5.5]
, it follows that
the object
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}F\in\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
can be
represented by a complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules concentrated in
the cohomological degrees
-l_{1}\leq m\leq l_{2}+t
with the terms
belonging to
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
.
This proves condition (IV) for the classes
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
and
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}

with respect to the pseudo-dualizing complex of
J
-torsion

S
-modules
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
∎

Corollary 13.4
.

In the context and assumptions of
Proposition
13.3
, for any conventional
or absolute derived category symbol
\star=\mathsf{b}
,
+
,
-
,

\varnothing
,
\mathsf{abs}+
,
\mathsf{abs}-
, or
\mathsf{abs}
, there is a triangulated
equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\star}(\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.

Proof.

This is a particular case of
Theorem
5.2
, which is applicable
in view of Theorem
13.1

and Proposition
13.3
.
∎

Notice that, according to
Remark
12.9
(2),
the quotflatness assumption can be dropped in
Proposition
13.3

and Corollary
13.4

if one is willing to assume the ring
S
to be Noetherian instead.

Let us also
warn
the reader that, unlike in the context
of
[
39
, diagram (14)]
, the square diagram formed by
the triangulated equivalences and triangulated forgetful functors

(21)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.47226pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.47226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern
