is usually
not commutative
.
In fact, the diagram of triangulated
functors (
21
) is commutative
when
J=SI
, but
not
in the general case.
This is clear from the proof of
Proposition
13.3
.

14.
Pseudo-Derived Categories in the Relative Context

Roughly speaking, a pseudo-coderived category is an intermediate
triangulated quotient category between the conventional derived and
the coderived category, while a pseudo-contraderived category is
an intermediate triangulated quotient category between the derived
and the contraderived category.
The concept of pseudo-derived categories presently works better in
the context of the Positselski co/contraderived categories than
the Becker ones, for the simple reason that we do not have a good
technology for proving that all Becker co/contraacyclic complexes in
an exact category are acyclic (cf. Lemma
2.1
).
That is why we only consider the Positselski co/contraderived
categories in this section.

We start with a brief recollection of the discussion of pseudo-derived
categories from
[
47
, Introduction and Section 4]

and
[
39
, Section 1]
.

Let
\mathsf{A}
be an exact category with exact functors of infinite direct
sum (as defined in Section
2
).
Then it is clear that every Positselski-coacyclic complex in
\mathsf{A}
is
acyclic (in the sense of
[
34
, Section A.1]
, which agrees with
the terminology in
[
28
,
6
]
when
\mathsf{A}
is idempotent-complete).
So
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{A})
, and it follows that the derived
category
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{A})
is naturally a triangulated Verdier quotient
category of
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})
.
In other words, the triangulated Verdier quotient functor

Q_{\mathsf{A}}\colon\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{A})
factorizes naturally through
the triangulated Verdier quotient functor
Q_{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathsf{co}}\colon\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})
, so
Q_{\mathsf{A}}
is the composition of
triangulated Verdier quotient functors

\textstyle{\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q_{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathsf{co}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R_{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathsf{co}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{A}).}

A triangulated category
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}
is said to be a
pseudo-coderived
category
of
\mathsf{A}
if it is endowed with two triangulated Verdier
quotient functors
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{A})
whose composition is the triangulated Verdier quotient
functor
R_{\mathsf{A}}^{\mathsf{co}}
.

Dually, let
\mathsf{B}
be an exact category with exact functors of
infinite products.
Then every Positselski-contraacyclic complex in
\mathsf{B}
is acyclic,

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{B})
.
So the triangulated Verdier quotient functor
Q_{\mathsf{B}}\colon\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{B})
factorizes naturally through
the triangulated Verdier quotient functor
Q_{\mathsf{B}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}\colon\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})
,

\textstyle{\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Q_{\mathsf{B}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R_{\mathsf{B}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{B}),}

and we obtain a natural triangulated Verdier quotient functor

R_{\mathsf{B}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}\colon\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{B})
.
A triangulated category
\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}
is said to be
a
pseudo-contraderived category
of
\mathsf{B}
if it is endowed
with two triangulated Verdier quotient functors
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{B})
whose composition
is the triangulated Verdier quotient functor
R_{\mathsf{B}}^{\mathsf{ctr}}
.

Now let
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathsf{A}
be a coresolving full subcategory closed
under infinite direct sums.
Then, by the dual version of
[
34
, Proposition A.3.1(b)]
,
the inclusion functor
\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
induces a triangulated
equivalence
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{E})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})
.
So we obtain a triangulated Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})
, and it follows
that the functor
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{A})
induced by the inclusion

\mathsf{E}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
is a triangulated Verdier quotient functor,
too
[
47
, Proposition 4.2(a)]
.
Thus
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})
is a pseudo-coderived category of
\mathsf{A}
,

\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{A})}

[
39
, Section 1]
.

Following the terminology in
[
39
, Section 8]
,
we will say that a complex in
\mathsf{A}
is
\mathsf{E}
-pseudo-coacyclic

if it is annihilated by the composition of triangulated Verdier
quotient functors
\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})
.
Denote the thick subcategory of
\mathsf{E}
-pseudo-coacyclic complexes
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{E}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{A})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})
.
So we have the inclusions of thick subcategories

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{E}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{A})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{A})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})

and a natural triangulated equivalence

(22)

\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})\simeq\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{E}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{A}).

Dually, let
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{B}
be a resolving full subcategory closed
under infinite products.
Then, by
[
34
, Proposition A.3.1(b)]
, the inclusion functor

\mathsf{F}\longrightarrow\mathsf{B}
induces a triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{F})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})
.
Hence
\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})
becomes a pseudo-contraderived category of
\mathsf{B}
,

\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{B})}

[
47
, Proposition 4.2(b)]
,
[
39
, Section 1]
.

We will say that a complex in
\mathsf{B}
is

\mathsf{F}
-pseudo-contraacyclic
if it is annihilated by
the composition of triangulated Verdier quotient functors
\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})
.
Denote the thick subcategory of
\mathsf{F}
-pseudo-contraacyclic complexes
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{F}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{B})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})
.
So we have the inclusions of thick subcategories

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{F}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{B})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{B})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})

and a natural triangulated equivalence

(23)

\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{F})\simeq\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{F}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{B}).

Lemma 14.1
.

(a)
If the exact category
\mathsf{E}
has finite homological
dimension, then the class of
\mathsf{E}
-pseudo-coacyclic complexes in

\mathsf{A}
coincides with the class of Positselski-coacyclic complexes,

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{E}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{A})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})
.

(b)
If the exact category
\mathsf{F}
has finite homological
dimension, then the class of
\mathsf{F}
-pseudo-contraacyclic complexes
in
\mathsf{B}
coincides with the class of Positselski-contraadyclic
complexes,
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{F}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{B})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})
.

Proof.

Let us prove part (a) (part (b) is dual).
By the definition, the
\mathsf{E}
-pseudo-coacyclic complexes in
\mathsf{A}

are the complexes annihilated by the composition of triangulated
Verdier quotient functors

\textstyle{\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E}).}

For an exact category
\mathsf{E}
of finite homological dimension with
exact functors of infinite direct sum, the classes of acyclic and
Positselski-coacyclic complexes coincide
[
33
, Remark 2.1]
,
so the functor
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{E})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})
is a triangulated
equivalence (in fact, isomorphism of triangulated categories).
∎

The following lemma is a category-theoretic generalization
of
[
39
, Lemma 8.3]
.

Lemma 14.2
.

(a)
Let
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{X}
be abelian categories with
exact functors of infinite direct sum and
\Theta\colon\mathsf{X}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
be
a faithful exact functor preserving infinite direct sums.
Let
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathsf{A}
be a coresolving subcategory closed under
infinite direct sums, and let
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}\subset\mathsf{X}
be the full
subcategory of all objects
E\in\mathsf{X}
such that
\Theta(E)\in\mathsf{E}
.
Assume that the full subcategory
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
is coresolving in
\mathsf{X}

(i. e., in other words, every object of
\mathsf{X}
is a subobject of
an object from
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
).
Then a complex
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
\mathsf{X}
belongs to the thick subcategory

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{X})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{X})
if and only if the complex

\Theta(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})
belongs to the full subcategory

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{E}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{A})
.

(b)
Let
\mathsf{B}
and
\mathsf{Y}
be abelian categories with
exact functors of infinite product and
\Theta\colon\mathsf{Y}\longrightarrow\mathsf{B}
be
a faithful exact functor preserving infinite products.
Let
\mathsf{F}\subset\mathsf{B}
be a resolving subcategory closed under
infinite products, and let
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}\subset\mathsf{Y}
be the full
subcategory of all objects
F\in\mathsf{Y}
such that
\Theta(F)\in\mathsf{F}
.
Assume that the full subcategory
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}
is resolving in
\mathsf{Y}

(i. e., in other words, every object of
\mathsf{X}
is a quotient object
of an object from
\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}
).
Then a complex
Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
\mathsf{Y}
belongs to the thick subcategory

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{Y})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Y})
if and only if the complex

\Theta(Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{B})
belongs to the full subcategory

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{F}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{B})
.

Proof.

Let us prove part (a).
The full subcategory
\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{E})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{E})
consists of all
complexes in
\mathsf{E}
that are acyclic in
\mathsf{A}
with the objects of
cocycles belonging to
\mathsf{E}
.
Similarly, the full subcategory
\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})\subset\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})

consists of all complexes in
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
that are acyclic in
\mathsf{X}
with
the objects of cocycles belonging to
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
.
Now a complex
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is acyclic in
\mathsf{X}
if and only if the complex

\Theta(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is acyclic in
\mathsf{A}
(since the functor of abelian
categories
\Theta\colon\mathsf{X}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
is exact and faithful).
Thus a complex
E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}
belongs to
\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})
if and
only if the complex
\Theta(E^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
belongs to
\mathsf{Ac}(\mathsf{E})
.

We have proved that the triangulated functor
\Theta\colon\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})
takes nonzero objects to nonzero objects (or in
other words, takes nonisomorphisms to nonisomorphisms, i. e., it is
conservative).
In order to prove the assertion of part (a), it remains to consider
the commutative diagram of triangulated functors

\textstyle{\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\textstyle{\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})}

implying that a complex
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
\mathsf{X}
is annihilated by
the triangulated Verdier quotient functor

\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{X})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{G}_{E})
if and only if the complex

\Theta(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is annihilated by the triangulated Verdier quotient
functor
\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{A})\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{E})
.
∎

15.
Semiderived Categories

The “semiderived categories” is an umbrella term for
the semicoderived and the semicontraderived categories.
The notion of the semiderived category goes back to
the book
[
33
]
.
Other sources relevant to our context include the paper
[
37
]
,
the book
[
43
]
, and the preprint
[
34
, Section 8]
.

Let
I
be an ideal in a commutative ring
R
, let
J
be an ideal
in a commutative ring
S
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring
homomorphism such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Denote by functor of restriction of scalars by
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}
.
We will use the same notation for functors of restriction of scalars
acting between the categories of torsion modules or contramodules,
that is
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

and
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
When the ideals
I\subset R
and
J\subset S
are finitely generated,
the latter functor preserves the quotseparatedness property of
contramodules, so we also have the forgetful functor

\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
.

A complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to be

Positselski-semicoacyclic
(
relative to
(R,I)
) if it is
Positselski-coacyclic as a complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules, i. e.,
if
\Theta(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
.
We denote the thick subcategory of Positselski-semicoacyclic complexes
by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
.
The
Positselski semicoderived category of
J
-torsion

S
-modules
(
relative to
(R,I)
) is defined as
the triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{sico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}).

The forgetful functor
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

is exact and preserves infinite direct sums, so it takes
Positselski-coacyclic complexes to Positselski-coacyclic complexes.
Hence any Positselski-coacyclic complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is
Positselski-semicoacyclic.
The forgetful functor
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

is also exact and faithful, so a complex
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

is acyclic if and only if the complex
\Theta(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is acyclic
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
The abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
has exact functors of infinite
direct sums, so all Positselski-coacyclic complexes in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

are acyclic.
It follows that any Positselski-semicoacyclic complex in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is acyclic.
So we have

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}).

Thus the Positselski semicoderived category is an intermediate
triangulated quotient category between the derived and the coderived
categories of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, i. e., the Positselski semicoderived
category is an example of a pseudo-coderived category of
J
-torsion

S
-modules in the sense of Section
14
.

Dual-analogously, a complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is said to be
Positselski-semicontraacyclic
(
relative to

(R,I)
) if it is Positselski-contraacyclic as a complex of

I
-contramodule
R
-modules, i. e., if

\Theta(Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
We denote the thick subcategory of Positselski-semicontraacyclic
complexes by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
The
Positselski semicontraderived category of
J
-contramodule

S
-modules
(
relative to
(R,I)
) is defined as
the triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

The forgetful functor
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

is exact and preserves infinite products, so it takes
Positselski-contraacyclic complexes to Positselski-contraacyclic
complexes.
Hence any Positselski-contraacyclic complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is
Positselski-semicontraacyclic.
The forgetful functor
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

is also exact and faithful, so a complex
Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

is acyclic if and only if the complex
\Theta(Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is acyclic
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
The abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
has exact functors of infinite
product, so all Positselski-contraacyclic complexes in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

are acyclic.
It follows that any Positselski-semicontraacyclic complex in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is acyclic.
So we have

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Thus the Positselski semicontraderived category is an intermediate
triangulated quotient category between the derived and the contraderived
categories of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
, i. e., the Positselski
semicontraderived category is an example of a pseudo-contraderived
category of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules in the sense of
Section
14
.

Assume that the ideals
I\subset R
and
J\subset S
are finitely
generated.
Then a complex of quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-modules

Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to be
Positselski-semicontraacyclic

(
relative to
(R,I)
) if it is Positselski-contraacyclic as
a complex of quotseparated
I
-contramodule
R
-modules, i. e., if

\Theta(Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})
.
We denote the thick subcategory of Positselski-semicontraacyclic
complexes by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})\subset\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})
.
The
Positselski semicontraderived category of quotseparated

J
-contramodule
S
-modules
(
relative to
(R,I)
) is
defined as the triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})=\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}).

All the arguments in the discussion above are applicable in
the case of quotseparated contramodules just as well.
So we have the inclusions of thick subcategories in the homotopy
category

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}).

Thus the Positselski semicontraderived category is an intermediate
triangulated quotient category between the derived and the contraderived
categories of
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
, i. e., the Positselski
semicontraderived category is an example of a pseudo-contraderived
category of quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-modules in the sense
of Section
14
.

The discussion of Becker semiderived categories requires more care.

Lemma 15.1
.

Let
I
be an ideal in a commutative ring
R
, let
J
be an ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism
such that
f(I)\subset J
.
In this setting:

(a)
The functor of restriction of scalars

\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
takes
Becker-coacyclic complexes in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
to Becker-coacyclic
complexes in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.

(b)
The functor of restriction of scalars

\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
takes
Becker-contraacyclic complexes in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
to
Becker-contraacyclic complexes in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.

(c)
Assume that the ideals
I\subset R
and
J\subset S

are finitely generated.
Then the functor of restriction of scalars

\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
takes
Becker-contraacyclic complexes in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
to
Becker-contraacyclic complexes in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
.

Proof.

Part (a): as the functor
\Theta
is exact and preserves infinite
direct sums (hence also all colimits), it suffices to refer to
the result of
[
43
, Lemma A.5]
, which is applicable to
Grothendieck abelian categories; or even directly
to
[
49
, Corollary 7.17]
.
Alternatively, one can refer to the more general result
of
[
34
, Lemma B.7.5(a)]
, and then one needs to know that
the forgetful functor
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
The point is that the desired right adjoint functor is easy to
construct explicitly: it takes an
I
-torsion
R
-module
M

to the
J
-torsion
S
-module
\Gamma_{J}(\Hom_{R}(S,M))
.

Parts (b–c): the argument is based on
[
34
, Lemma B.7.5(b)]
.
In both the cases (b) and (c), the respective functor
\Theta
is
exact and preserves infinite products (hence all limits).
So it remains to show that the functor
\Theta
has a left adjoint.
One can observe that all the contramodule categories in question
are locally
\lambda
-presentable, and the functor
\Theta

preserves
\lambda
-directed colimits for a suitable regular
cardinal
\lambda
(
\lambda=\aleph_{1}
in part (c) and in the case
of finitely generated ideals
I\subset R
and
J\subset S
in part (b)).
See the discussion in Section
1
.
Hence a left adjoint functor to
\Theta
exists by
[
1
, Adjoint
Functor Theorem 1.66]
.

Alternatively, the left adjoint functors can be constructed explicitly.
In the context of part (b), the left adjoint functor to

\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
takes
an
I
-contramodule
R
-module
P
to the
J
-contramodule

S
-module
\Delta_{J}(S\otimes_{R}P)
.
In the context of part (c), the left adjoint functor to
the left adjoint functor to
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
takes a quotseparated
I
-contramodule

R
-module
P
to the quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-module

\mathbb{L}_{0}\Lambda_{J}(S\otimes_{R}P)
.
See Section
1
for the notation.
∎

Let
I
be an ideal in a commutative ring
R
, let
J
be an ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism
such that
f(I)\subset J
.
A complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to be

Becker-semicoacyclic
(
relative to
(R,I)
) if it is
Becker-coacyclic as a complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules, i. e.,
if
\Theta(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bco}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
.
We denote the thick subcategory of Becker-semicoacyclic complexes by

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
.
The
Becker semicoderived category of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

(
relative to
(R,I)
) is defined as the triangulated Verdier
quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}).

The forgetful functor
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

takes Becker-coacyclic complexes to Becker-coacyclic complexes by
Lemma
15.1
(a).
Hence any Becker-coacyclic complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is
Becker-semicoacyclic.
A complex
X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is acyclic if and only if
the complex
\Theta(X^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is acyclic in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
,
as explained in the first half of this section.
The abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
has enough injective objects,
so all Becker-coacyclic complexes in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
are acyclic
by Lemma
2.1
(a).
It follows that any Becker-semicoacyclic complex in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
is acyclic.
Thus we have

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bco}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}).

Dual-analogously, a complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

is said to be
Becker-semicontraacyclic
(
relative to

(R,I)
) if it is Becker-contraacyclic as a complex of

I
-contramodule
R
-modules, i. e., if

\Theta(Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
We denote the thick subcategory of Positselski-semicontraacyclic
complexes by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
The
Becker semicontraderived category of
J
-contramodule

S
-modules
(
relative to
(R,I)
) is defined as
the triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

The forgetful functor
\Theta\colon S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}\longrightarrow R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}

takes Becker-contraacyclic complexes to Becker-contraacyclic complexes
by Lemma
15.1
(b).
Hence any Becker-contraacyclic complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is
Becker-semicontraacyclic.
A complex
Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is acyclic if and only if
the complex
\Theta(Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})
is acyclic in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
,
as explained in the first half of this section.
The abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
has enough projective objects
(see Section
1
), so all Becker-contraacyclic
complexes in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
are acyclic by
Lemma
2.1
(b).
It follows that any Becker-semicontraacyclic complex in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
is acyclic.
Thus we have

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Assume that the ideals
I\subset R
and
J\subset S
are finitely
generated.
Then a complex of quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-modules

Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is said to be
Becker-semicontraacyclic

(
relative to
(R,I)
) if it is Becker-contraacyclic as
a complex of quotseparated
I
-contramodule
R
-modules, i. e., if

\Theta(Y^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}})\in\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})
.
We denote the thick subcategory of Becker-semicontraacyclic
complexes by
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})\subset\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})
.
The
Becker semicontraderived category of quotseparated

J
-contramodule
S
-modules
(
relative to
(R,I)
)
is defined as the triangulated Verdier quotient category

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})=\mathsf{K}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}).

Similarly to the arguments above, one proves the inclusions of
thick subcategories in the homotopy category

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bctr}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}})\subset\mathsf{Ac}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}).

Lemma
15.1
(c) is
relevant here.

16.
Relative Dualizing Complexes
and Semico-Semicontra Correspondence

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent.
Let
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a dualizing complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules,
as defined in Section
10

(cf. Theorem
10.3
).

We will need to make the assumption that the injective dimensions of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules (as objects of the abelian
category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) are finite.
For some results, we will also need the assumption that the projective
dimensions of contraflat
I
-contramodule
R
-modules (as objects
of the abelian category
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
) are finite.
See the discussion in Section
10
.

Let
J
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
S
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism
such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that
S
is a flat
R
-module.

As in Section
13
, we consider the dual Koszul
complex
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})
for some finite sequence of
generators
\mathbf{s}
of the ideal
J\subset S
.
Let
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a finite complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules isomorphic
to
K^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}_{\infty}(S,\mathbf{s})\otimes_{R}D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{b}}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}})
.
We will say that
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
is a
relative dualizing complex

for the morphism of ring-ideal pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
.

The following theorem is the second main result of this paper.

Theorem 16.1
.

Let
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a relative dualizing complex for a morphism of
ring-ideal pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
corresponding to a dualizing
complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, as defined above.
The assumptions above are enforced; so the ideals
I\subset R

and
J\subset S
are finitely generated and weakly proregular,
the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent, and
S
is a flat
R
-module.
Assume further that the morphism of pairs
(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is
quotflat in the sense of Section
12
, and that
the injective dimensions of fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules
(as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) are finite.
Then there is a triangulated equivalence between the Becker
semicoderived and semicontraderived categories (defined in
Section
15
)

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.

The proof of Theorem
16.1

is based on two propositions.
Let us start with introducing notation.
Denote by
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

the full subcategory of
J
-torsion
S
-modules that are

fp-injective as
I
-torsion
R
-modules
.
Similarly, denote by
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}\subset S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
the full subcategory of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules
that are
contraflat as
I
-contramodule
R
-modules
.

Proposition 16.2
.

Let
I
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
R

such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent, let
J
be a finitely
generated ideal in a commutative ring
S
, and let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S

be a ring homomorphism such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is quotflat.
Then the inclusion of exact/abelian categories

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
induces
a triangulated equivalence between the conventional derived
category and the Becker semicoderived category

\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}).

Proof.

First we observe that, for any complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules

M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, there exists a complex
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}
together with a morphism of
complexes of
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with a cone belonging
to
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
.
Indeed, the result of
Theorem
2.2
(a) for
the abelian category
\mathsf{A}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
essentially says that
for any complex
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
there exists a complex
of injective objects
H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
together with
a morphism of complexes of
S
-modules
M^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow H^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
whose
cone is Becker-coacyclic in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
.
It remains to point out that all injective
J
-torsion
S
-modules
are fp-injective as
I
-torsion
R
-modules by
Proposition
12.3
, and all
Becker-coacyclic complex of
J
-torsion
S
-modules are
Becker-coacyclic as complexes of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
by Lemma
15.1
(a).

Now the well-known result of
[
23
, Proposition 10.2.7(i)]

or
[
34
, Lemma A.3.3(b)]
is applicable, and it remains to show
that a complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}
is acyclic
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}
if and only if it belongs to

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
.
Notice that a complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}
is
acyclic in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}
if and only if its
underlying complex of
R
-modules is acyclic in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
(see the first paragraph of the proof
of Lemma
14.2
(a)).
So it remains to point that a complex in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}

is acyclic in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}
if and only if it is
Becker-coacyclic in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
, by
Proposition
11.2
(b)
and its proof.
∎

Proposition 16.3
.

Let
I
be a weakly proregular finitely generated ideal in
a commutative ring
R
such that the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent,
let
J
be a finitely generated ideal in a commutative ring
S
, and
let
f\colon R\longrightarrow S
be a ring homomorphism such that
f(I)\subset J
.
Assume that the morphism of pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is quotflat.
Then the inclusion of exact/abelian categories

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}\longrightarrow S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
induces
a triangulated equivalence between the conventional derived
category and the Becker semicontraderived category

\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Proof.

This proposition is dual-analogous to the previous one, and the proof
is also dual-analogous.
First we observe that, for any complex of
J
-contramodule

S
-modules
P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
, there exists a complex
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}
together with a morphism of complexes
of
S
-modules
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
with a cone belonging
to
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
Indeed, the result of
Theorem
2.2
(b) for
the abelian category
\mathsf{B}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
essentially says that
for any complex
P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
there exists a complex
of projective objects
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
together with
a morphism of complexes of
S
-modules
F^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\longrightarrow P^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
whose
cone is Becker-contraacyclic in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
It remains to point out that all projective
J
-contramodule

S
-modules are contraflat as
I
-contramodule
R
-modules by
Proposition
12.4
, and all
Becker-contraacyclic complex of
J
-contramodule
S
-modules are
Becker-coacyclic as complexes of
I
-contramodule
R
-modules by
Lemma
15.1
(b) or (c).

Now the well-known result of
[
23
, Proposition 10.2.7(ii)]

or
[
34
, Lemma A.3.3(a)]
is applicable, and it remains to show
that a complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}
is acyclic
in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}
if and only if it belongs to

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
.
Notice that a complex in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}
is
acyclic in
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}
if and only if its
underlying complex of
R
-modules is acyclic in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
(cf. the first paragraph of the proof
of Lemma
14.2
(a)).
So it remains to point that a complex in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}

is acyclic in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}
if and only if it is
Becker-contraacyclic in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I\mathsf{ctrfl}}
, by
Proposition
11.3
(b)
and its proof.

Let us mention that the assumption of weak proregularity of the ideal

I\subset R
is needed in the argument above because it is used in
the proof of Proposition
12.4
.
Besides, Proposition
12.4
is
only applicable to quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-modules.
The assumption of weak proregularity of the ideal
J\subset S

is only used in the argument above in order to claim that all

J
-contramodule
S
-modules are quotseparated,

S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
Without the weak proregularity assumption on the finitely generated
ideal
J\subset S
, the assertion of the present proposition holds in
the context of quotseparated
J
-contramodule
S
-modules, i. e.,
for the abelian category
S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{qs}}
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem
16.1
.

The desired triangulated equivalence is constructed as the composition
of triangulated equivalences

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}})\\
\simeq\mathsf{D}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Here the first and the third triangulated equivalences are provided by
Propositions
16.2

and
16.3
, respectively.
The middle triangulated equivalence is obtained as a particular case
of Corollary
13.4
.
Let us spell out the details.

In the context of Section
13
, we put
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
,
and consider the full subcategories
\mathsf{E}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{F}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
By Proposition
11.1
,
the pair of classes
\mathsf{E}
and
\mathsf{F}
satisfies
conditions (I–IV) from Section
5

for the dualizing complex of
R
-torsion
I
-modules
L^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}=D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}

(that is where the assumption of finite injective dimension of
fp-injective
I
-torsion
R
-modules is used).
Then, in the notation of Section
13
, we have

\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-fpinj}}}}
and

\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{(R,I){\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctrfl}}}}
, and
Corollary
13.4

(for
\star=\varnothing
) is applicable.
∎

Our final theorem is the version of
Theorem
16.1
for
the Positselski semiderived categories instead of the Becker ones.

Theorem 16.4
.

Let
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
be a relative dualizing complex for a morphism of
ring-ideal pairs
f\colon(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
corresponding to a dualizing
complex of
I
-torsion
R
-modules
D^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}
.
The assumptions from the beginning of this section are enforced; so
the ideals
I\subset R
and
J\subset S
are finitely generated and
weakly proregular, the ring
R
is
I
-adically coherent, and
S

is a flat
R
-module.
Assume that the morphism of pairs
(R,I)\longrightarrow(S,J)
is
quotflat in the sense of Section
12
.
Assume further that the injective dimensions of fp-injective

I
-torsion
R
-modules (as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
) are finite,
and the projective dimensions of contraflat
J
-contramodule

R
-modules (as objects of
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
) are finite.
Then there is a triangulated equivalence between the Positselski
semicoderived and semicontraderived categories (defined in
Section
15
)

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{sico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\simeq\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})

provided by (appropriately defined) mutually inverse derived functors

\mathbb{R}\Hom_{S}(U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}},{-})
and
U^{\text{\smaller\smaller$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}\otimes_{S}^{\mathbb{L}}{-}
.

Proof.

Under the finite injective/projective dimension assumptions of
the present theorem, the Positselski-coacyclicity property of complexes
in
R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
agrees with the Becker-coacyclicity by
Proposition
11.2
(c), and
the Positselski-contraacyclicity property of complexes in

R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
agrees with the Becker-contraacyclicity by
Proposition
11.3
(c).
Hence we have
\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{sico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})
and

\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})=\mathsf{D}^{\mathsf{bsictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}})
, and the present theorem is
a particular case of
Theorem
16.1
.

Alternatively, here is a direct proof based on the results
of Section
14
.
In the notation of the proof of
Theorem
16.1
, the exact
categories
\mathsf{E}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}^{\mathsf{fpinj}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}

and
\mathsf{F}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}^{\mathsf{ctrfl}}\subset R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
have finite
homological dimensions in our present assumptions.
In the context of Section
14
, put

\mathsf{A}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{B}=R{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{I{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
.
Then we have
\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{E}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{A})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{co}}(\mathsf{A})
and

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{F}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{B})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{ctr}}(\mathsf{B})
by
Lemma
14.1
.

Put
\mathsf{X}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}}
and
\mathsf{Y}=S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}
, and denote
by
\Theta
both the forgetful functors
\mathsf{X}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}
and

\mathsf{Y}\longrightarrow\mathsf{B}
(as in Section
15
).
Then Lemma
14.2
tells us that

\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{X})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sico}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-tors}}}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{Y})=\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{sictr}}_{(R,I)}(S{\operatorname{\mathsf{--Mod}}}_{J{\operatorname{\mathsf{-ctra}}}}).

Following the discussion in Section
14
,
we have triangulated equivalences

\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{X})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psco}}}}(\mathsf{X})\simeq\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Y})/\mathsf{Ac}^{\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}{\operatorname{\mathsf{-psctr}}}}(\mathsf{Y})\simeq\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}}).

It remains to refer to
Corollary
13.4

(for
\star=\varnothing
) for the triangulated equivalence

\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{G}_{\mathsf{E}})\simeq\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{H}_{\mathsf{F}})
.
∎

Finally, let us reiterate the comments from the discussion at
the end of Section
13
.
Firstly, according to Remark
12.9
(2),
the quotflatness assumption can be dropped in
Theorems
16.1

and
16.4
if one assumes
the ring
S
to be Noetherian.
In Propositions
16.2

and
16.3
, the quotflatness
assumption can be replaced by the assumption that the
R
-module

S
is flat if the ring
S
is Noetherian.

Secondly, we reiterate the
warning
that, unlike in the context
of

39
, diagram (15)]
, the square diagram

)

)

, but

.
